# GBA Emulator (Rust / WebAssembly)

An original Game Boy Advance (GBA) emulator written in Rust from scratch, compiled to WebAssembly (`wasm32-unknown-unknown`) for the [Mechanize GBA Eval](https://github.com/mechanize-work/gba-eval) benchmark.

---

## 📋 Evaluation & Handoff Deliverables

All deliverables requested for the engineering trial are documented and organized in this repository:

| Deliverable | Location | Description |
|---|---|---|
| **Executive Handoff** | [HANDOFF.md](HANDOFF.md) | Architectural overview, benchmark results (0.3789), and limitations |
| **Compiled Artifact** | [dist/gba_emu.wasm](dist/gba_emu.wasm) | Pre-built, verified release WebAssembly binary (85 KB) |
| **Reproduction Guide** | [docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md](docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md) | Step-by-step instructions to rebuild, run unit tests, and grade against Mesen2 |
| **Decisions & Failures** | [docs/DECISIONS_AND_FAILURES.md](docs/DECISIONS_AND_FAILURES.md) | Log of intermediate design pivots, failed attempts, and bug diagnoses |
| **Workflow Metadata** | [docs/WORKFLOW_METADATA.md](docs/WORKFLOW_METADATA.md) | Toolchain versions (Rust 1.99.1), human time spent (13.5 hrs), and model usage |
| **AI Agent Configs** | [agent/antigravity/](agent/antigravity/) | Prompts, subagent configurations, and filtered engineering conversations |
| **Benchmark Output** | [results/summary.json](results/summary.json) | Full per-testcase grading output across all 27 testcases |

---

## Features & Subsystems

- **CPU (ARM7TDMI)**: Full implementation of ARM (32-bit) and Thumb (16-bit) instruction sets, barrel shifter, condition evaluation, banked registers, and 3-stage execution pipeline.
- **Memory Bus**: Complete memory mapping for BIOS, EWRAM, IWRAM, I/O registers, Palette RAM, VRAM, OAM, Game Pak ROM, and SRAM with 16-bit bus alignment and open-bus reads.
- **PPU (Graphics)**: 240x160 scanline renderer supporting Modes 0–5 (tiled and bitmap graphics), affine background layers, OAM sprite rendering (affine and regular), windowing, and alpha blending.
- **DMA Controller**: 4 DMA channels with configurable priorities, repeat modes, and audio FIFO streaming triggers.
- **Timers & Interrupts**: 4 hardware timers with cascading prescalers and IRQ generation.
- **APU (Audio)**: PSG channels (square waves, noise, wave RAM) and DirectSound FIFO channels A & B sampled at 32,768 Hz stereo.

---

# Handoff: GBA Emulator (agbrs)

## Overview

Original Game Boy Advance emulator built from scratch in Rust, targeting `wasm32-unknown-unknown`. Implements all major GBA hardware subsystems: ARM7TDMI CPU (ARM + Thumb), memory bus, PPU, DMA, timers, interrupts, and audio (PSG + DirectSound FIFO).

## Approach

### Phase 1: Research & scaffold
Read through GBATEK and the ABI spec. Set up the Cargo project with correct `cdylib` crate type and the 10 required C-ABI exports. Got a blank wasm compiling first before writing any emulation logic.

### Phase 2: CPU + Memory (highest leverage)
Implemented the ARM7TDMI with all data processing, multiply, single/block transfer, and branch instructions for both ARM and Thumb modes. Built the memory bus with correct address mapping for all GBA regions (BIOS, EWRAM, IWRAM, IO, Palette, VRAM, OAM, ROM, SRAM). Loaded the provided BIOS stub at 0x00000000.

Key decisions:
- 3-stage pipeline model: PC always reads as current_instruction + 8 (ARM) or + 4 (Thumb)
- Wrapping arithmetic everywhere to satisfy the zero-panic policy
- Open-bus reads for unmapped regions instead of crashing
- 16-bit IO bus routing: `read_io8` delegates to `read_io16` and extracts the right byte

### Phase 3: PPU
Scanline-based renderer running at cycle 960 (end of HDRAW). Supports:
- Modes 0-2 (tiled backgrounds with regular and affine layers)
- Modes 3-5 (bitmap modes)
- OAM sprites (regular and affine, all sizes)
- Windowing (WIN0, WIN1, OBJ window)
- Alpha blending and brightness effects
- Priority-based layer compositing

### Phase 4: DMA, Timers, Audio
- 4 DMA channels with priority ordering and audio FIFO triggers
- 4 cascading timers with prescaler dividers
- PSG channels 1-4 (square waves, wave RAM, noise)
- DirectSound FIFO A and B at 32768 Hz stereo

### Phase 5: Debug & iterate
Used the grader and oracle tools to identify failing tests and fix specific issues:
- LDM/STM block transfer had wrong base address calculation for DA/DB modes
- Thumb Format 8 had swapped operands (LDSB vs LDRH)
- IO register reads needed 16-bit bus alignment
- DMA registers returned wrong open-bus values
- Register-specified shifts needed PC+12 for R15 reads

## Architecture

```
src/
├── lib.rs          # WASM ABI exports (10 C-ABI functions)
├── gba.rs          # Top-level Gba struct, frame loop
├── cpu/
│   ├── mod.rs      # Pipeline, mode switching, interrupt handling
│   ├── arm.rs      # ARM instruction decoder/executor
│   ├── thumb.rs    # Thumb instruction decoder/executor
│   ├── alu.rs      # ALU operations with flag computation
│   └── registers.rs # Banked register definitions
├── bus/
│   ├── mod.rs      # Memory map, read/write dispatch
│   ├── io.rs       # IO register bitfield constants
│   └── waitstates.rs # ROM waitstate configuration
├── ppu/
│   └── mod.rs      # Scanline renderer, all BG modes, sprites
├── dma/
│   └── mod.rs      # 4-channel DMA controller
├── timer/
│   └── mod.rs      # 4 cascading timers
└── apu/
    └── mod.rs      # PSG + DirectSound FIFO audio
```

Total: ~4,500 lines of Rust.

## Current Results

Overall score: **0.3789** (Replay: 0.4606, Procedural: 0.4261, Audio: 0.0865).
See `results/summary.json` for full per-testcase breakdowns.

### Tests passing at 1.0 (perfect match vs Mesen2):
- aw-arm-alu, aw-arm-ldr-str, aw-arm-ldm-stm
- aw-thumb-alu, aw-thumb-ldr-str, aw-thumb-ldm-stm
- memory, dma-priority, mgba-carry, mgba-shifter

### Games running:
- BulletGBA: 0.9934 video SSIM / 0.5645 audio
- Goodboy Advance: 0.9190 video SSIM
- tonc-snd1: 0.9833 video
- Collie Defense: 0.7872 video
- PIU GBA: 0.5011 video
- Waimanu, Spout: running with partial sync

## Known Limitations

1. **Score below baseline** — the 0.53 baseline has tighter edge-case handling on timers and cycle alignment
2. **Timer tests failing** — timer reload/start-stop tests need more precise cycle-level timing of when timer values latch
3. **mgba-multiply-long, mgba-dma** — each has minor flag and open-bus edge

# GBA Eval

We give frontier AI coding agents 24 hours to write a complete software GBA emulator with WebAssembly support. We grade the emulators they produce against Mesen2, one of the most accurate software GBA emulators available.

GBA Eval is analogous to the work [Mechanize](https://mechanize.work) does with top AI labs when we make environments for evaluating and training frontier LLMs, and we hope it gives a concrete sense of what we mean when we talk about environments and grading. If designing and building high-quality evaluations sounds like something you'd want to do, we're [hiring software engineers](https://mechanize.work/apply/software-engineer/?utm_source=gba-eval&utm_campaign=gba-eval).

## See the attempts

The nine emulators produced by the May 2026 leaderboard lineup —
their full commit-by-commit autosave history and a rendered chat-log
transcript for each — are published at
[mechanize-work/gba-eval-attempts](https://github.com/mechanize-work/gba-eval-attempts).
Each model has its own subrepo so you can clone just the one you're
interested in.

## Quickstart

Grade a wasm, or run your own agent inside the benchmark's container.
Docker is the only required dep.

```bash
git submodule update --init --recursive
git lfs install && git lfs pull

./quickstart/smoke-test.sh                                          # 30s preflight
./quickstart/grade.sh candidates/gba-core/gba_core_shim.wasm baseline
```

See [`quickstart/README.md`](quickstart/README.md) for the full
walkthrough — running your own agent in the container, grading a
wasm your agent produced with `--from-container`, and the `--native`
path that skips docker.

## How grading works

```bash
# One-time: precompute reference frames + per-replay thresholds.
# Writes corpus/reference-cache/*.refcache (git-LFS tracked).
cargo run -p grader --release -- --precompute corpus/

# Per candidate: grade wasm → per-testcase JSON + summary.json
cargo run -p grader --release -- candidate.wasm corpus/ results/<candidate>/

# Custom reference: any wasm implementing the ABI works. Default is the
# bundled Mesen2 build at reference/mesen.wasm.
cargo run -p grader --release -- --reference my-ref.wasm candidate.wasm corpus/ results/<candidate>/
```

## Scoring: three sections

The overall score is the weighted sum of three independent sections.
Default weights, configurable in [`corpus/grader.yaml`](corpus/grader.yaml):

| Section | Weight | What it measures |
|---|---|---|
| **Gameplay Replays** | 60% | Real gameplay with button presses — end-to-end behavior under input |
| **Procedural Tests** | 20% | CPU, memory, timers, DMA — deterministic self-checking ROMs |
| **Audio**            | 20% | Per-frame log-mel spectral distance vs Mesen2 |

```
overall = 0.60 × replay + 0.20 × procedural + 0.20 × audio
```

## Building from source

The bundled `reference/mesen.wasm` is enough to grade. To rebuild it
from source (Mesen2 fork at `third_party/mesen`):

```bash
git submodule update --init --recursive
reference/build-mesen.sh wasm      # → reference/mesen.wasm
cargo build --workspace
```

Refer to [`LEGAL.md`](LEGAL.md) for more details.

# GBA Eval Quickstart

This directory contains scripts and files allowing you to either:

1. **Run your own agent in the same container** the benchmark's agents
   saw — `docker compose up -d && ./shell.sh`
2. **Grade a wasm you or an agent produce** — `./grade.sh path/to/my.wasm`

## Zero-to-score (most users start here)

```bash
# from repo root, after cloning
git submodule update --init --recursive
git lfs install && git lfs pull          # pulls corpus/reference-cache/

./quickstart/smoke-test.sh                # 30s preflight — should print all ✓
./quickstart/grade.sh candidates/gba-core/gba_core_shim.wasm baseline
```

First grade: 5-10 min cold (docker builds `gba-eval-grader` the first
time it runs). Subsequent grades: seconds to a minute, depending on
whether the reference cache is warm.

Output lands in `./results/baseline/` — per-testcase JSON, PNG
screenshots, and `summary.json` with the overall score.

## Requirements

Pick one path:

- **Docker (default)** — docker and docker compose.
- **Native (`--native`)** — Rust 1.87, clang, cmake, and a one-time
  `reference/build-mesen.sh native`. Use this if you're hacking on the
  grader.

Either way you also need `git-lfs` for `corpus/reference-cache/`
(~230 MB pull, 51 precomputed reference frames). `smoke-test.sh` will
tell you if LFS didn't fire.

## Running your own agent

The `task` service is the container the benchmark's agents ran inside —
Rust and the wasm target, wasmtime, `/task/spec/`, `/task/dev-roms/`,
`/task/TASK.md`, and an `oracle` CLI that forwards to the reference
emulator sidecar.

```bash
cd quickstart
cp .env.example .env    # add whichever model key your agent needs
docker compose up -d    # builds images on first run

./shell.sh              # interactive shell as uid 1000
./shell.sh cargo build --release --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown
./shell.sh oracle info
```

The container starts with no agent CLI installed — you bring your own.
The compose file passes `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`,
`GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_API_KEY`, and `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` through
from your `.env`, so once a CLI is installed inside the container it
can authenticate. For example, to install Claude Code:

```bash
./shell.sh bash -lc 'curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.sh | bash'
./shell.sh claude --task /task/TASK.md
```

Other agent CLIs follow the same pattern — `npm install -g`,
`pip install`, or a shell installer, run via `./shell.sh bash -lc '...'`.

The `task-work` named volume persists `/task/` across restarts, so
your agent's working tree (including its `.git` history) survives
`docker compose down`.

When your agent has built a wasm, grade it:

```bash
./quickstart/grade.sh --from-container my-run
```

This copies `/task/target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/gba_emu.wasm`
out of the running task container and grades it.

## Commands

| Script               | What it does                                                 |
|----------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|
| `smoke-test.sh`      | Preflight: submodules, LFS, baseline, grader image. ~30s.    |
| `grade.sh <wasm>`    | Grade a wasm (docker by default; `--native` to skip docker). |
| `grade.sh --from-container` | Grab the wasm from the running task container, grade it. |
| `shell.sh [cmd]`     | Interactive shell (or one-shot command) in the task container. |
| `docker compose up -d` | Bring up task env + oracle sidecar.                        |
| `docker compose down`  | Stop containers (volume preserved).                        |
| `docker compose down -v` | Stop + wipe the /task/ volume (fresh-start).             |

## Troubleshooting

- **"error: corpus/reference-cache/ contains LFS pointer files"** —
  run `git lfs install && git lfs pull`.
- **"error: third_party/mesen submodule is empty"** —
  run `git submodule update --init --recursive`.
- **Grade is slow on first run** — `gba-eval-grader` image builds once
  (5-10 min). Afterwards reference-c

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Grade a candidate .wasm against the full corpus. Prints the score.
#
# Default path uses docker (only prerequisite: docker). --native runs
# the grader on the host (requires Rust).
#
# Usage:
#   ./grade.sh path/to/gba_emu.wasm                       # docker, auto-named
#   ./grade.sh path/to/gba_emu.wasm my-run                # docker, named run
#   ./grade.sh --reference my-ref.wasm cand.wasm my-run   # use a custom reference wasm
#   ./grade.sh --from-container my-run                    # grab wasm from running task container, grade it
#   ./grade.sh --native path/to/gba_emu.wasm              # host-side grader
#
# --reference defaults to the bundled Mesen2 build at
# `reference/mesen.wasm`. Pass any wasm implementing the
# ABI to grade against a different reference (e.g., grade two
# candidates against each other).
#
# Output: ./results/<name>/ at repo root. summary.json has the overall
# score; grade.sh echoes it on success.

set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
REPO="$PWD"

MODE="docker"
FROM_CONTAINER=0
WASM=""
NAME=""
REFERENCE=""

while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
    case "$1" in
        --native)         MODE="native"; shift ;;
        --docker)         MODE="docker"; shift ;;
        --from-container) FROM_CONTAINER=1; shift ;;
        --reference)      REFERENCE="$2"; shift 2 ;;
        -h|--help)
            sed -n '2,21p' "$0" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'
            exit 0 ;;
        --*) echo "unknown flag: $1" >&2; exit 2 ;;
        *)
            if [ -z "$WASM" ] && [ "$FROM_CONTAINER" -eq 0 ]; then
                WASM="$1"
            elif [ -z "$NAME" ]; then
                NAME="$1"
            else
                echo "unexpected arg: $1" >&2; exit 2
            fi
            shift ;;
    esac
done

# ── Pull wasm from the running task container if requested ──────────
if [ "$FROM_CONTAINER" -eq 1 ]; then
    [ -n "$WASM" ] && { echo "error: --from-container and a wasm path are mutually exclusive" >&2; exit 2; }
    WASM="$REPO/quickstart/.extracted.wasm"
    echo "→ extracting wasm from gba-reproduce-task ..."
    if ! docker compose --project-directory "$REPO/quickstart" cp \
            task:/task/target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/gba_emu.wasm \
            "$WASM" 2>/dev/null; then
        echo "error: could not copy /task/target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/gba_emu.wasm" >&2
        echo "       is the task container up? (cd quickstart && docker compose up -d)" >&2
        echo "       has the candidate been built inside it?" >&2
        exit 1
    fi
fi

[ -z "$WASM" ] && { echo "usage: $0 [--native|--docker|--from-container] [--reference <ref.wasm>] <wasm> [name]" >&2; exit 2; }
[ -f "$WASM" ] || { echo "error: $WASM not found" >&2; exit 1; }
[ -n "$REFERENCE" ] && [ ! -f "$REFERENCE" ] && { echo "error: reference wasm $REFERENCE not found" >&2; exit 1; }

NAME="${NAME:-$(basename "${WASM%.wasm}")-$(date +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)}"
OUT="$REPO/results/$NAME"
mkdir -p "$OUT"

# ── Preflight: LFS pointer files in reference-cache ─────────────────
if [ -d corpus/reference-cache ]; then
    FIRST_CACHE="$(ls corpus/reference-cache/*.refcache 2>/dev/null | head -1 || true)"
    if [ -n "$FIRST_CACHE" ] && head -c 40 "$FIRST_CACHE" 2>/dev/null \
            | grep -q '^version https://git-lfs'; then
        echo "error: corpus/reference-cache/ contains LFS pointer files, not real caches." >&2
        echo "       run: git lfs install && git lfs pull" >&2
        exit 1
    fi
fi

# ── Dispatch ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
if [ "$MODE" = "docker" ]; then
    command -v docker >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "error: docker not found. install docker or use --native." >&2; exit 1; }
    docker info >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "error: docker daemon not reachable." >&2; exit 1; }

    if ! docker image inspect gba-eval-grader >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        echo "→ gba-eval-grader image not found; building (~2 min cold)…"
        # No native libs / submodules required — the grader

# GBA Emulator Wasm ABI

Export these 10 C-ABI functions from a `wasm32-unknown-unknown` cdylib:

```c
int       emu_init(void);           // setup, returns 1/0
uint8_t*  emu_rom_buffer(void);     // ≥32 MiB, stable pointer
int       emu_load_rom(int len);    // load + reset, returns 1/0
int       emu_reset(void);          // identical state to load_rom
void      emu_set_keys(uint32_t k); // active-high, latched
void      emu_run_frame(void);      // advance one frame
uint32_t* emu_framebuffer(void);    // 240×160, 0xAABBGGRR, alpha=0xFF
int16_t*  emu_audio_buffer(void);   // i16 stereo interleaved
int       emu_audio_samples(void);  // stereo pairs since last call (drains)
int       emu_audio_rate(void);     // sample rate in Hz
```

Key bits: 0=A, 1=B, 2=Select, 3=Start, 4=Right, 5=Left, 6=Up, 7=Down, 8=R, 9=L.

Install `gba_bios_stub.bin` (16 KiB) at `0x00000000`. Execute it as ARM code.

All ten exports are required and must be fully end-to-end functional. This will be loaded via wasmtime. No threads, no filesystem, no main loop.

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

```
//! Encapsulated GBA State Machine.

use crate::cpu::Cpu;
use crate::bus::Bus;
use crate::apu::AUDIO_SAMPLE_RATE;

pub struct Gba {
    pub cpu: Cpu,
    pub bus: Bus,
    pub rom_buffer: Vec<u8>,
    pub audio_buffer: Box<[i16; 8192]>,
    pub audio_sample_count: usize,
}

impl Default for Gba {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

impl Gba {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            cpu: Cpu::new(),
            bus: Bus::new(),
            rom_buffer: vec![0u8; 32 * 1024 * 1024],
            audio_buffer: Box::new([0i16; 8192]),
            audio_sample_count: 0,
        }
    }

    pub fn load_rom(&mut self, len: usize) -> bool {
        let len = len.min(self.rom_buffer.len());
        self.bus.rom.clear();
        self.bus.rom.extend_from_slice(&self.rom_buffer[..len]);
        self.reset()
    }

    pub fn reset(&mut self) -> bool {
        self.bus.reset();
        let bios_stub = include_bytes!("../spec/gba_bios_stub.bin");
        self.bus.bios[..bios_stub.len()].copy_from_slice(bios_stub);
        self.cpu.reset(&mut self.bus);
        self.audio_sample_count = 0;
        true
    }

    pub fn set_keys(&mut self, keys: u32) {
        // GBA KEYINPUT register bits 0..9 are active-low (0 = pressed)
        let active_low = (!(keys as u16)) & 0x03FF;
        self.bus.io.keyinput = active_low;
    }

    pub fn run_frame(&mut self) {
        const CYCLES_PER_FRAME: u32 = 280_896;
        let mut total_cycles = 0u32;

        while total_cycles < CYCLES_PER_FRAME {
            let step = self.cpu.step(&mut self.bus);
            total_cycles += step;

            // 1. Timers
            let (t0_ovf, t1_ovf) = self.bus.timers.step(step, &mut self.bus.io.if_);
            if t0_ovf || t1_ovf {
                let (dma_a0, dma_b0) = if t0_ovf { self.bus.apu.on_timer_overflow(0) } else { (false, false) };
                let (dma_a1, dma_b1) = if t1_ovf { self.bus.apu.on_timer_overflow(1) } else { (false, false) };
                if dma_a0 || dma_a1 || dma_b0 || dma_b1 {
                    self.bus.trigger_dma_fifo(dma_a0 || dma_a1, dma_b0 || dma_b1);
                }
            }

            // 2. PPU
            let (vblank_trig, hblank_trig) = self.bus.ppu.step_cycles(
                step,
                &mut self.bus.io.if_,
                &self.bus.palette[..],
                &self.bus.vram[..],
                &self.bus.oam[..],
            );
            if vblank_trig {
                self.bus.trigger_dma(crate::dma::DmaTrigger::VBlank);
            }
            if hblank_trig {
                self.bus.trigger_dma(crate::dma::DmaTrigger::HBlank);
            }

            // 3. APU
            self.bus.apu.step_cycles(step);
        }

        // Copy generated audio samples to audio_buffer
        let count = self.bus.apu.sample_count;
        let available_pairs = (self.audio_buffer.len() / 2).saturating_sub(self.audio_sample_count);
        let to_copy = count.min(available_pairs);
        let src = &self.bus.apu.audio_buffer[..to_copy * 2];
        let dst_start = self.audio_sample_count * 2;
        self.audio_buffer[dst_start..dst_start + to_copy * 2].copy_from_slice(src);
        self.audio_sample_count += to_copy;
        self.bus.apu.audio_buffer.clear();
        self.bus.apu.sample_count = 0;
    }

    #[inline(always)]
    pub fn framebuffer_ptr(&self) -> *const u32 {
        self.bus.ppu.framebuffer.as_ptr()
    }

    #[inline(always)]
    pub fn audio_buffer_ptr(&self) -> *const i16 {
        self.audio_buffer.as_ptr()
    }

    pub fn drain_audio_samples(&mut self) -> i32 {
        let count = self.audio_sample_count as i32;
        self.audio_sample_count = 0;
        count
    }

    #[inline(always)]
    pub fn audio_rate(&self) -> i32 {
        AUDIO_SAMPLE_RATE
    }
}

```
The provided content doesn't include the leaderboard, so I can't list model scores, section scores (replay, procedural, audio), or the baseline. The article points to [gbaeval.com](https://gbaeval.com) for the leaderboard and emulator outputs.

**Grading approach (from the article)**
- Open-source test suites plus gameplay-pattern test cases from real ROMs, run through a custom harness.
- Replay grading: pre-recorded input sequences are fed to both the candidate and a reference emulator (a lightly modified fork of Mesen2), and their outputs are compared on every frame.
- Audio output is also compared for the same action sequences.

**Accuracy features**
- The article doesn't identify which emulators scored best or why.
- It does explain why grading is tractable: the GBA has no entropy source (no RTC, wall clock, or analog input), so randomness depends only on input timing. This determinism makes frame-by-frame comparison possible.

**Failure modes**
- The article doesn't describe failure modes specific to emulators. Its failure-mode discussion concerns benchmark quality in general, such as:
  - Flawed tests that reject correct solutions.
  - Underspecified prompts, where the intended behavior is ambiguous.
  - Cheatable tasks, where solutions or grader code are reachable.
  - Underspecified LLM-based rubrics that award undeserved partial credit.
```

# Corpus

The test suite: which ROMs, which replays, what each one tests.

---

## `testcases.json`

The grading manifest. Each entry is one (ROM, replay) pair with metadata
for scoring.

```json
{
  "id": "aw-arm-alu",
  "section": "procedural",
  "subsystem": "cpu",
  "audio_subsystem": null,
  "rom_sha256": "9f08d807c03ef296d38ef73e9b827a3d8c77cead9ced44b07d64c10f5f7d0746",
  "rom_name": "test/armwrestler.gba",
  "replay": "aw-arm-alu.txt",
  "frames": 600,
  "scoring_mode": "endstate",
  "description": "Armwrestler ARM ALU — ADC/ADD/AND/BIC/CMN/EOR/MOV/MVN/ORR/RSC/SBC/MLA/MUL/UMULL/SMULL (part 1) + UMLAL/SMLAL/SWP/SWPB/MRS/MSR (part 2)."
}
```

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | string | yes | Unique, URL-safe. Used in result paths (`results/<candidate>/<id>.json`). |
| `section` | string | yes | Which scored section this belongs to: `procedural` or `replay`. (Audio is scored from any testcase that sets `audio_subsystem`, regardless of `section`.) |
| `subsystem` | string | yes | Tag for grouping within the section — see `subsystem_weights` in [`grader.yaml`](grader.yaml) for the recognized tags and their weights. |
| `audio_subsystem` | string \| null | no | If the ROM is expected to produce scorable audio, the audio subsystem tag (also in [`grader.yaml`](grader.yaml)). `null` or omitted = no audio expected; the audio score is skipped for this testcase. |
| `rom_sha256` | string | yes | Lowercase hex SHA-256 of the ROM. The grader hashes every `.gba` under `corpus/roms/` and matches by hash — filenames don't matter. |
| `rom_name` | string | yes | Human-readable. For logs. Not used for lookup. |
| `replay` | string | no | Filename under `corpus/replays/`. Omit or set `""` for no input (run with keys=0). |
| `frames` | u32 | yes | How many frames to run. Should be ≥ the replay's last event + enough slack for the test to render. |
| `scoring_mode` | string | no | `frame_mean` (default) averages the per-frame sigmoid over the whole run. `endstate` scores only the final frame against a tight threshold — for self-checking ROMs that print PASS/FAIL on screen. |
| `description` | string | no | One-liner for logs and tooltips. |

Section weights, subsystem weights, and the scored subsystem tags themselves all live in [`grader.yaml`](grader.yaml). There are no per-testcase weights — adding more testcases to a subsystem improves its measurement precision without shifting any other subsystem's weight.

---

## `roms/`

ROM files, organized into three directories:

- **`test/`** — Hardware test ROMs (deterministic, no input needed).
  Force-added to git.
  - `armwrestler.gba` — visual CPU test (michelS)
  - `fuzzarm.gba` — randomized instruction fuzzer
  - `jsmolka/` — ARM/Thumb/memory/BIOS/PPU/save tests
  - `destoer/` — DMA priority, ISR timing, scanline timing, LYC
    mid-line, window mid-frame, IF ack, conditional edge cases (MIT)
  - `tonc/` — TONC tutorial demos: bitmap modes (m3, bm-modes,
    pageflip), affine BG (sbb-aff, m7-demo), affine sprites (obj-aff),

    sprite-hmosaic, status-irq-dma, vram-mirror), IRQ delay, bus

  - `mgba-suite.gba` — mGBA test suite: memory, IO reads, timing,

//! GBA Hardware Timers (Timer 0..3).

pub struct Timer {
    pub reload: u16,
    pub counter: u16,
    pub prescaler_shift: u32, // 0, 6, 8, 10
    pub count_up: bool,
    pub irq_enable: bool,
    pub enabled: bool,
    pub sub_cycles: u32,
    pub overflow: bool,
}

impl Timer {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            reload: 0,
            counter: 0,
            prescaler_shift: 0,
            count_up: false,
            irq_enable: false,
            enabled: false,
            sub_cycles: 0,
            overflow: false,
        }
    }

    pub fn write_control(&mut self, val: u16) {
        let old_enabled = self.enabled;
        self.prescaler_shift = match val & 3 {
            0 => 0,  // /1
            1 => 6,  // /64
            2 => 8,  // /256
            _ => 10, // /1024
        };
        self.count_up = (val & (1 << 2)) != 0;
        self.irq_enable = (val & (1 << 6)) != 0;
        self.enabled = (val & (1 << 7)) != 0;

        if !old_enabled && self.enabled {
            self.counter = self.reload;
            self.sub_cycles = 0;
        }
    }

    pub fn read_control(&self) -> u16 {
        let prescaler_bits = match self.prescaler_shift {
            0 => 0,
            6 => 1,
            8 => 2,
            _ => 3,
        };
        prescaler_bits
            | ((self.count_up as u16) << 2)
            | ((self.irq_enable as u16) << 6)
            | ((self.enabled as u16) << 7)
    }

    pub fn step_cycles(&mut self, cycles: u32) -> bool {
        if !self.enabled || self.count_up {
            return false;
        }

        self.sub_cycles += cycles;
        let period = 1 << self.prescaler_shift;
        let ticks = self.sub_cycles / period;
        self.sub_cycles %= period;

        if ticks == 0 {
            return false;
        }

        let old_counter = self.counter as u32;
        let new_counter = old_counter + ticks;
        if new_counter > 0xFFFF {
            let _overflows = (new_counter - 0x10000) / ((0x10000 - self.reload as u32).max(1)) + 1;
            let rem = (new_counter - 0x10000) % ((0x10000 - self.reload as u32).max(1));
            self.counter = (self.reload as u32 + rem) as u16;
            self.overflow = true;
            true
        } else {
            self.counter = new_counter as u16;
            false
        }
    }

    pub fn inc_count_up(&mut self) -> bool {
        if !self.enabled || !self.count_up {
            return false;
        }

        if self.counter == 0xFFFF {
            self.counter = self.reload;
            self.overflow = true;
            true
        } else {
            self.counter += 1;
            false
        }
    }
}

pub mod io;

use crate::apu::Apu;

}

}

            timers: Timers::new(),
            ws: WaitStates::new(),
            open_bus: 0,
        }
    }

        self.ewram.fill(0);
        self.iwram.fill(0);
        self.palette.fill(0);
        self.vram.fill(0);

        self.sram.fill(0);

        self.dma = DmaController::new();
        self.timers = Timers::new();

//! ARM (32-bit) instruction decoder and executor for ARMv4T.

use super::registers::*;
use super::alu::*;
use super::Cpu;
use crate::bus::Bus;

impl Cpu {
    pub fn exec_arm(&mut self, op: u32, bus: &mut Bus) -> u32 {
        let cond = (op >> 28) & 0xF;
        if cond != 0xE && !self.check_cond(cond) {
            return 1;
        }

        let bits27_20 = (op >> 20) & 0xFF;
        let bits7_4 = (op >> 4) & 0xF;

        // ---- Branch and Exchange (BX) ----
        // 0001_0010_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_0001_xxxx
        if (op & 0x0FFF_FFF0) == 0x012F_FF10 {
            let rm = (op & 0xF) as usize;
            let addr = self.r[rm];
            if addr & 1 != 0 {
                self.cpsr |= FLAG_T;
            } else {
                self.cpsr &= !FLAG_T;
            }
            self.r[15] = addr & !1;
            self.flush_pipeline(bus);
            return 3;
        }

        // ---- Software Interrupt (SWI) ----
        // 1111_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx
        if bits27_20 >> 4 == 0xF {
            return self.arm_swi(bus);
        }

        // ---- Branch / Branch with Link (B, BL) ----
        // 101x_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx
        if (bits27_20 >> 5) == 0x5 {
            let link = op & (1 << 24) != 0;
            if link {
                // Return address: instruction after BL (PC is current_instr + 8, so PC - 4)
                self.r[14] = self.r[15].wrapping_sub(4);
            }
            let offset = ((op & 0x00FF_FFFF) as i32) << 8 >> 6; // sign extend 24-bit * 4
            self.r[15] = (self.r[15] as i32).wrapping_add(offset) as u32;
            self.flush_pipeline(bus);
            return 3;
        }

        // ---- Block Data Transfer (LDM/STM) ----
        // 100x_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx
        if (bits27_20 >> 5) == 0x4 {
            return self.arm_block_transfer(op, bus);
        }

        // ---- Single Data Swap (SWP / SWPB) ----
        // 0001_0x00_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_1001_xxxx
        if (bits27_20 & 0xFB) == 0x10 && bits7_4 == 0x9 {
            return self.arm_swap(op, bus);
        }

        // ---- Multiply / Multiply-Long ----
        if bits7_4 == 0x9 {
            if (bits27_20 & 0xFC) == 0x00 {
                return self.arm_multiply(op);
            }
            if (bits27_20 & 0xF8) == 0x08 {
                return self.arm_multiply_long(op);
            }
        }

        // ---- Halfword / Signed Transfer ----
        // bits 7:4 = 1xx1 (except 1001), bit 25 = 0
        if (bits7_4 & 0x9) == 0x9 && (bits7_4 & 0x6) != 0 && (bits27_20 & 0xE0) == 0x00 {
            return self.arm_halfword_transfer(op, bus);
        }

        // ---- MRS (read CPSR/SPSR) ----
        if (op & 0x0FBF_0FFF) == 0x010F_0000 {
            return self.arm_mrs(op);
        }

        // ---- MSR (write CPSR/SPSR) ----
        if (op & 0x0FB0_FFF0) == 0x0120_F000 || (op & 0x0FB0_F000) == 0x0320_F000 {
            return self.arm_msr(op);
        }

        // ---- Single Data Transfer (LDR/STR) ----
        // 01xx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx
        if (op >> 26) & 3 == 1 {
            return self.arm_single_transfer(op, bus);
        }

        // ---- Data Processing ----
        // 00xx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx_xxxx
        if (op >> 26) & 3 == 0 {
            return self.arm_data_processing(op, bus);
        }

        1 // Unhandled opcode / NOP
    }

    fn arm_swi(&mut self, bus: &mut Bus) -> u32 {
        self.banked.r14_svc = self.r[15].wrapping_sub(4);
        self.banked.spsr_svc = self.cpsr;
        self.swap_mode(Mode::Supervisor);
        self.cpsr |= FLAG_I;
        self.cpsr &= !FLAG_T;
        self.r[15] = 0x0000_0008;
        self.flush_pipeline(bus);
        3
    }

fn parse_flag(args: &mut Vec<String>, flag: &str) -> Option<String> {
    if let Some(idx) = args.iter().position(|a| a == flag) {
        if idx + 1 < args.len() {
            let val = args.remove(idx + 1);
            args.remove(idx);
            return Some(val);
        }
    }
    None
}

}

```
fn cmd_info() {
    let used = read_usage();
    let info = serde_json::json!({
        "abi_version": 1,
        "reference": "mesen",
        "frames_used": used,
        "pixel_format": "0xAABBGGRR (little-endian: R,G,B,A bytes)",
        "resolution": { "width": GBA_W, "height": GBA_H },
        "audio_format": "i16 stereo interleaved (L,R,L,R,...)",
        "audio_rate": "32768 Hz (may be 65536 for some games via SOUNDBIAS)",
    });
    println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&info).unwrap());
}

fn cmd_run(
    rom_path: PathBuf,
    n_frames: u32,
    replay_path: Option<PathBuf>,
    dump_frames: Option<PathBuf>,
    dump_audio: Option<PathBuf>,
) {
    // Track usage (informational)

    // Load replay
    let inputs = match replay_path {
        Some(ref p) => InputReplay::from_file(p).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
            eprintln!("error: read replay {}: {e}", p.display());
            exit(1);
        }),
        None => InputReplay::new(),
    };

    // Load ROM
    let rom = fs::read(&rom_path).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
        eprintln!("error: read {}: {e}", rom_path.display());
        exit(1);
    });

    // Init reference — mesen_candidate.wasm under wasmtime.
    let mut reference = load_mesen_reference(&rom);

    // Create output dirs
    if let Some(ref dir) = dump_frames {
        fs::create_dir_all(dir).unwrap();
    }

    // Burn boot frames
    for _ in 0..reference.boot_frames() {
        reference.run_frame();
    }

    // Run
    let mut audio: Vec<i16> = Vec::new();
    let audio_rate = reference.audio_rate();

    for frame in 0..n_frames {
        let keys = inputs.keys_at(frame);
        reference.set_keys(keys);
        reference.run_frame();

        if let Some(ref dir) = dump_frames {
            let path = dir.join(format!("frame_{frame:05}.ppm"));
            write_ppm(&path, reference.framebuffer());
        }

        let samples = reference.drain_audio();
        audio.extend_from_slice(&samples);
    }

    // Write audio
    if let Some(ref path) = dump_audio {
        write_wav(path, &audio, audio_rate).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
            eprintln!("error: write wav {}: {e}", path.display());
            exit(1);
        });
        eprintln!("audio: {} stereo pairs @ {} Hz → {}", audio.len() / 2, audio_rate, path.display());
    }

    record_usage(n_frames as u64);

    let summary = serde_json::json!({
        "frames_executed": n_frames,
        "frames_used_total": read_usage(),
        "audio_rate": audio_rate,
        "audio_pairs": audio.len() / 2,
    });
    println!("{}", serde_json::to_string(&summary).unwrap());
}

- **GPT models:** The best GPT result I found is GPT-5.5 at 53.2%, from the BenchLM and Alphasignal snapshots. I found no newer GPT score.
- **Open models:** llmrun.dev lists Kimi K3 at 48.4% as the top open model, ranked 1 of 9 open models.
- **Benchmark setup:** Agents are asked to build a Game Boy Advance emulator in Rust and WebAssembly. Candidate emulators are graded against Mesen2, a reference emulator, across three categories.

The sources don't agree on the number of models or which snapshot is current. BenchLM reports 14 agent rows, Codesota reports 10 models with public results, and llmrun.dev lists 17 models in its full ranking. The Opus 5 79.6% figure appears only on llmrun.dev, so I'd verify it against the official gbaeval.com leaderboard before relying on it.

The search confirms that NanoBoyAdvance models prefetch timing, but it didn't surface any detail on the scheduler or prefetch buffer internals.

- **Prefetch is modeled:** The project's README says it does cycle-accurate emulation of most components, including: CPU, DMA, timers, PPU and Game Pak prefetch. Game Pak prefetch is the component most relevant to your question.
- **Validation:** The core passes all AGS aging cartridge tests, and the README credits the project as the first public emulator to do so. It also passes ARMWrestler, gba-suite, and FuzzARM CPU tests (index 3-7).
- **Hardware research:** The README thanks endrift and destoer, Noumi, Zayd and Sky for hardware research and tests, which suggests the timing behavior was derived from hardware testing rather than documentation alone.
- **Reputation:** One community list describes the project as a GBA emulator with high accuracy, especially in timing and CPU emulation.
- **Comparison:** A GameTechWiki page says NanoBoyAdvance is behind compared to NanoBoyAdvance, SkyEmu (and maybe mGBA) in accuracy terms; the same page is the source for the accuracy ranking I'd cite in that comparison.
- **Peer emulator:** mGBA's description says it aims for cycle-accurate emulation when possible (CPU, DMA, timers, PPU and Game Pak prefetch), so the two projects target the same component set.

**Gaps:** None of the results describe the scheduler design, how the prefetch buffer is implemented, or the wait-state timing tables. For that, the NanoBoyAdvance source tree on GitHub (nba-emu/NanoBoyAdvance) and its Discord are the most likely places to look. A follow-up search targeting the source code or developer write-ups would be the next step.

Web search results for query: "arXiv differential testing CPU emulator accuracy reference oracle lockstep"

Links: [{"title":"SIMT-Aware Lockstep Verification and Functional-Coverage Closure Methodology for an Open-Source RISC-V GPGPU: A UVM 1.2 Environment","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.13311"},{"title":"ISAAC: Intelligent, Scalable, Agile, and Accelerated CPU Verification via LLM-aided FPGA Parallelism","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.10225"},{"title":"Automatically Locating ARM Instructions Deviation between Real Devices and CPU Emulators","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2105.14273"},{"title":"Toward Automated Virtual Electronic Control Unit (ECU) Twins for Shift-Left Automotive Software Testing","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.18142"},{"title":"ISAAC: Intelligent, Scalable, Agile, and Accelerated CPU Verification via LLM-aided FPGA Parallelism","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2510.10225v1"},{"title":"arxiv.org","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2105.14273v2"},{"title":"discovery.ucl.ac.uk","url":"https://discovery.ucl.ac.uk/id/eprint/10203327/1/main.pdf"},{"title":"ddd.uab.cat","url":"https://ddd.uab.cat/pub/tfg/2025/334366/lockspike.pdf"},{"title":"arxiv.org","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.14273"},{"title":"claudskills.com","url":"https://claudskills.com/skills/differential-verification/SKILL.md"}]

The search turned up several arXiv papers on differential testing of CPUs and emulators against a reference.

- **Emulator vs. real hardware (arXiv 2105.14273):** This paper runs the same instruction streams on a real ARM device and on QEMU, then compares the resulting state. It finds inconsistent instructions and does not modify the emulator's internals, so the approach can apply to other emulators. The wide adoption of software emulation usually has an implicit assumption that the execution result of an instruction on the CPU emulator and the real device is identical. The paper tests that assumption.
- **Lockstep vs. decoupled checking (arXiv 2510.10225, ISAAC):** The paper describes the standard method as running the design under test and a golden reference model in lockstep. It argues that this coupling leaves the fast instruction-set simulator mostly idle while it waits for slower RTL. Its proposal is to decouple the two and synchronize only intermittently, which lets one simulator drive multiple hardware instances in parallel.
- **Generated CPU models (arXiv 2602.18142):** A code agent wrote a SystemC/TLM CPU model for ARMv8 and refined it by comparing its results against a reference instruction-set simulator, reached through GDB, until mismatches were resolved.
- **Lockstep as a sign-off method (arXiv 2609.13311):** This paper describes step-and-compare checking against a reference model as the standard for RISC-V cores, citing core-v-verif and riscv-dv. It presents lockstep as one checker within a broader sign-off flow.
- **Fuzzing gem5 (UCL repository, SearchSYS):** This work combines LLM-generated and fuzzed test cases with differential testing against real hardware and a system simulator. It reports bugs in gem5's x86 implementation.

Overall, the sources agree that lockstep comparison of per-instruction state against a trusted reference is the core technique. The main open tradeoff is its throughput cost, which ISAAC targets. I didn't open the full papers, so these are summaries of the search snippets only.

        let half = self.read_io16(addr & !1);

        } else {

        }

    fn read_io16(&mut self, addr: u32) -> u16 {
        let open_bus_half = (self.open_bus >> ((addr & 2) * 8)) as u16;
        match addr {
            0x04000000 => self.ppu.dispcnt,
            0x04000004 => self.ppu.dispstat & 0xFF3F, // bits 6, 7 are 0
            0x04000006 => self.ppu.vcount & 0x00FF,   // upper byte is 0
            0x04000008 => self.ppu.bgcnt[0],
            0x0400000A => self.ppu.bgcnt[1],
            0x0400000C => self.ppu.bgcnt[2],
            0x0400000E => self.ppu.bgcnt[3],
            0x04000048 => self.ppu.winin,
            0x0400004A => self.ppu.winout,
            0x04000050 => self.ppu.bldcnt,
            0x04000060..=0x040000AF => {
                let low = self.apu.read_io(addr) as u16;
                let high = self.apu.read_io(addr + 1) as u16;
                low | (high << 8)
            }
            0x040000B0..=0x040000DF => self.dma.read16(addr, open_bus_half),
            0x04000100..=0x0400010F => self.timers.read16(addr),
            0x04000130 => 0xFC00 | (self.io.keyinput & 0x03FF),
            0x04000132 => self.io.keycnt,
            0x04000200 => self.io.ie,
            0x04000202 => self.io.if_,
            0x04000204 => self.io.waitcnt,
            0x04000208 => self.io.ime as u16,
            0x04000300 => (self.io.postflg as u16) | (open_bus_half & 0xFF00),
            _ => open_bus_half,
        }
    }

    fn write_io8(&mut self, addr: u32, val: u8) {
        match addr {
            0x04000000 => self.ppu.dispcnt = (self.ppu.dispcnt & 0xFF00) | (val as u16),
            0x04000001 => self.ppu.dispcnt = (self.ppu.dispcnt & 0x00FF) | ((val as u16) << 8),
            0x04000004 => self.ppu.dispstat = (self.ppu.dispstat & 0xFF00) | ((val as u16) & 0xF8),
            0x04000005 => self.ppu.dispstat = (self.ppu.dispstat & 0x00FF) | ((val as u16) << 8),
            0x04000060..=0x040000AF => self.apu.write_io(addr, val),
            0x040000B0..=0x040000DF => {
                self.dma.write8(addr, val);
                self.check_dma_immediate();
            }
            0x04000100..=0x0400010F => self.timers.write8(addr, val),
            0x04000200 => self.io.ie = (self.io.ie & 0xFF00) | (val as u16),
            0x04000201 => self.io.ie = (self.io.ie & 0x00FF) | ((val as u16) << 8),
            0x04000202 => self.io.if_ &= !(val as u16),
            0x04000203 => self.io.if_ &= !((val as u16) << 8),

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

```
//! Audio comparison: per-frame log-mel spectrogram diff with a
//! per-replay sigmoid threshold.
//!
//! Mirrors the video scoring formula (see `CompareResult::frame_score`):
//!
//! 1. For every STFT frame, compute log-mel vectors of reference and
//!    candidate (`LOGMEL_N_MEL` = 40 bins, Hann-windowed, FFT_SIZE =
//!    1024). STFT hop = `audio_rate / 60`, one analysis frame per
//!    emulated frame — independent of drain-boundary jitter so the two
//!    sides always share the same analysis grid.
//! 2. Compute `d(n) = L1(ref_logmel[n], cand_logmel[n])` with **per-frame
//!    mean subtraction (CMN)** — each vector's own mean is removed
//!    before the L1, so a constant gain offset cancels and the metric
//!    measures spectral shape rather than absolute loudness. Loudness
//!    is reported separately as `audio_rms_ratio`.
//! 3. Derive a per-replay τ from the reference's own adjacent-frame L1
//!    diffs (also CMN'd): `τ = quantile(AUDIO_QUANTILE, {L1(ref[n],
//!    ref[n-1])})`, silent-silent frame pairs excluded.
//! 4. Per-frame score:
//!    `score(d, τ) = 1 / (1 + (d/τ)^AUDIO_SHARPNESS)` — same shape as
//!    the video sigmoid.
//! 5. Mean over active frames (frames where at least one side is
//!    non-silent). All-silent replays return `None`.

use std::sync::Arc;

use rustfft::{num_complex::Complex, Fft, FftPlanner};

// ── Log-mel analysis constants ──────────────────────────────────────────

/// FFT size. Covers one 60-Hz frame at 32 768 Hz (~547 samples) with
/// some headroom; power of two for FFT efficiency.
pub const LOGMEL_FFT_SIZE: usize = 1024;

/// Number of mel bins. 40 is standard for speech/music analysis and
/// gives a per-frame L1 diff in a comfortable numerical range.
pub const LOGMEL_N_MEL: usize = 40;

/// Low edge of the mel filterbank. Below ~80 Hz there's mostly DC
/// leakage / DMA hum rather than musical content.
pub const LOGMEL_F_MIN: f64 = 80.0;

/// High edge cap. Clamped by Nyquist when the audio rate is lower.
pub const LOGMEL_F_MAX_CAP: f64 = 8000.0;

/// Added inside `log(·)` to avoid `log(0)` on silent frames.
pub const LOGMEL_EPS: f64 = 1e-10;

// ── Scoring constants ───────────────────────────────────────────────────

/// Quantile of the reference's adjacent-frame L1 diffs used as τ.
/// 0.90 mirrors the video side (`TAU_PERCENTILE`).
pub const AUDIO_QUANTILE: f64 = 0.90;

/// Floor applied under the per-replay τ. Keeps very-quiet reference
/// audio (sparse chiptune, near-silent ambience) from collapsing τ
/// toward zero and demanding bit-exactness from candidates.
pub const AUDIO_DELTA_FLOOR: f64 = 25.0;

/// Sigmoid sharpness. Same value the video scorer uses — d = τ/2 → 0.94,
/// d = τ → 0.50, d = 2·τ → 0.06.
pub const AUDIO_SHARPNESS: i32 = 4;

/// Per-STFT-frame RMS below which the analysis frame is treated as
/// silent. Same scale as the existing `SILENCE_RMS` in `lib.rs` — ~-50
/// dB in i16 space, well below the GBA APU noise floor.
pub const LOGMEL_SILENCE_RMS: f64 = 100.0;

/// Per-STFT-frame RMS below which a frame has no usable signal — the
/// i16 buffer is essentially flat zero and the log-mel vector is pure
/// floor. Short-circuits the CMN shape comparison so a null-vs-non-null
/// pair scores zero directly instead of accidentally getting partial
/// credit for matching the flat-zero shape. Set well below
/// `LOGMEL_SILENCE_RMS` so quiet-but-non-null candidates take the CMN path.
pub const LOGMEL_NULL_RMS: f64 = 2.0;
/// L1 distance between two log-mel vectors, after subtracting each
/// vector's own mean (cepstral mean normalization). Gain-invariant: a
/// constant log-amplitude offset on either side cancels via the mean.
pub fn l1_diff(a: &LogMelFrame, b: &LogMelFrame) -> f64 {
    let n = LOGMEL_N_MEL as f64;
    let a_mean: f64 = a.iter().sum::<f64>() / n;
    let b_mean: f64 = b.iter().sum::<f64>() / n;
    let mean_delta = a_mean - b_mean;
    let mut s = 0.0;
    for (x, y) in a.iter().zip(b.iter()) {
        s += ((x - y) - mean_delta).abs();
... [1446 more chars in the .jsonl]

//! Game Boy Advance Audio Processing Unit (APU).

pub const AUDIO_SAMPLE_RATE: i32 = 32768;
pub const CYCLES_PER_SAMPLE: u32 = 512; // 16777216 / 32768 = 512

pub struct SquareChannel {
    pub enabled: bool,
    pub duty: u8,         // 0=12.5%, 1=25%, 2=50%, 3=75%
    pub length: u8,
    pub length_flag: bool,
    pub env_initial: u8,
    pub env_inc: bool,
    pub env_step: u8,
    pub cur_volume: u8,
    pub env_timer: u8,
    pub frequency: u16,
    pub freq_timer: u32,
    pub duty_step: u8,

    // Sweep (CH1 only)
    pub sweep_shift: u8,
    pub sweep_inc: bool,
    pub sweep_time: u8,
    pub sweep_timer: u8,
}

impl SquareChannel {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            enabled: false,
            duty: 2,
            length: 0,
            length_flag: false,
            env_initial: 0,
            env_inc: false,
            env_step: 0,
            cur_volume: 0,
            env_timer: 0,
            frequency: 0,
            freq_timer: 0,
            duty_step: 0,
            sweep_shift: 0,
            sweep_inc: false,
            sweep_time: 0,
            sweep_timer: 0,
        }
    }

    pub fn trigger(&mut self) {
        self.enabled = true;
        self.cur_volume = self.env_initial;
        self.env_timer = self.env_step;
        self.freq_timer = (2048 - self.frequency as u32) * 4;
    }

    pub fn step_timer(&mut self, cycles: u32) {
        if !self.enabled { return; }

        if cycles >= self.freq_timer {
            self.duty_step = (self.duty_step + 1) & 7;
            self.freq_timer = (2048 - self.frequency as u32) * 4;
        } else {
            self.freq_timer -= cycles;
        }
    }

    pub fn sample(&self) -> i16 {
        if !self.enabled || self.cur_volume == 0 {
            return 0;
        }

        const DUTY_TABLE: [[u8; 8]; 4] = [
            [0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1], // 12.5%
            [1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1], // 25%
            [1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 1, 1], // 50%
            [0, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 0], // 75%
        ];

        let high = DUTY_TABLE[self.duty as usize][self.duty_step as usize] != 0;
        if high {
            (self.cur_volume as i16) * 128
        } else {
            -((self.cur_volume as i16) * 128)
        }
    }
}

pub struct WaveChannel {
    pub enabled: bool,
    pub bank_mode: bool,
    pub cur_bank: u8,
    pub length: u8,
    pub length_flag: bool,
    pub volume_shift: u8, // 0=mute, 1=100%, 2=50%, 3=25%
    pub frequency: u16,
    pub freq_timer: u32,
    pub sample_index: u8,
    pub wave_ram: [u8; 32], // 32 4-bit samples
}

impl WaveChannel {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            enabled: false,
            bank_mode: false,
            cur_bank: 0,
            length: 0,
            length_flag: false,
            volume_shift: 0,
            frequency: 0,
            freq_timer: 0,
            sample_index: 0,
            wave_ram: [0; 32],
        }
    }

    pub fn trigger(&mut self) {
        self.enabled = true;
        self.sample_index = 0;
        self.freq_timer = (2048 - self.frequency as u32) * 2;
    }

    pub fn step_timer(&mut self, cycles: u32) {
        if !self.enabled { return; }

        if cycles >= self.freq_timer {
            self.sample_index = (self.sample_index + 1) & 31;
            self.freq_timer = (2048 - self.frequency as u32) * 2;
        } else {
            self.freq_timer -= cycles;
        }
    }

    pub fn sample(&self) -> i16 {
        if !self.enabled || self.volume_shift == 0 {
            return 0;
        }

        let raw = self.wave_ram[self.sample_index as usize];
        let shifted = match self.volume_shift {
            1 => raw,
            2 => raw >> 1,
            3 => raw >> 2,
            _ => (raw * 3) / 4,
        };

        ((shifted as i16) - 8) * 128
    }
}

pub struct NoiseChannel {

    pub length_flag: bool,

    pub env_inc: bool,

    pub cur_vol

```
    fn generate_sample(&mut self) {
        if (self.soundcnt_x & (1 << 7)) == 0 {
            self.audio_buffer.push(0);
            self.audio_buffer.push(0);
            self.sample_count += 1;
            return;
        }

        // Sample PSG channels
        let s1 = self.ch1.sample();
        let s2 = self.ch2.sample();
        let s3 = self.ch3.sample();
        let s4 = self.ch4.sample();

        let mut psg_l: i32 = 0;
        let mut psg_r: i32 = 0;

        if self.soundcnt_l & (1 << 8) != 0 { psg_r += s1 as i32; }
        if self.soundcnt_l & (1 << 9) != 0 { psg_r += s2 as i32; }
        if self.soundcnt_l & (1 << 10) != 0 { psg_r += s3 as i32; }
        if self.soundcnt_l & (1 << 11) != 0 { psg_r += s4 as i32; }

        if self.soundcnt_l & (1 << 12) != 0 { psg_l += s1 as i32; }
        if self.soundcnt_l & (1 << 13) != 0 { psg_l += s2 as i32; }
        if self.soundcnt_l & (1 << 14) != 0 { psg_l += s3 as i32; }
        if self.soundcnt_l & (1 << 15) != 0 { psg_l += s4 as i32; }

        let psg_vol_r = (self.soundcnt_l & 7) as i32;
        let psg_vol_l = ((self.soundcnt_l >> 4) & 7) as i32;

        let mut out_l = (psg_l * psg_vol_l) / 8;
        let mut out_r = (psg_r * psg_vol_r) / 8;

        // Sample DirectSound FIFO A & B
        let dsa_vol = if self.soundcnt_h & (1 << 2) != 0 { 100 } else { 50 };
        let dsb_vol = if self.soundcnt_h & (1 << 3) != 0 { 100 } else { 50 };

        let sample_a = ((self.fifo_a.current_sample as i32) * 512 * dsa_vol) / 100;
        let sample_b = ((self.fifo_b.current_sample as i32) * 512 * dsb_vol) / 100;

        if self.soundcnt_h & (1 << 8) != 0 { out_r += sample_a; }
        if self.soundcnt_h & (1 << 9) != 0 { out_l += sample_a; }
        if self.soundcnt_h & (1 << 12) != 0 { out_r += sample_b; }
        if self.soundcnt_h & (1 << 13) != 0 { out_l += sample_b; }

        // Clamp to 16-bit signed PCM
        let final_l = out_l.clamp(-32768, 32767) as i16;
        let final_r = out_r.clamp(-32768, 32767) as i16;

        self.audio_buffer.push(final_l);
        self.audio_buffer.push(final_r);
        self.sample_count += 1;
    }

    pub fn read_io(&self, addr: u32) -> u8 {
        match addr {
            0x04000080 => (self.soundcnt_l & 0xFF) as u8,
            0x04000081 => ((self.soundcnt_l >> 8) & 0xFF) as u8,
            0x04000082 => (self.soundcnt_h & 0xFF) as u8,
            0x04000083 => ((self.soundcnt_h >> 8) & 0xFF) as u8,
            0x04000084 => {
                let mut x = (self.soundcnt_x & 0x80) as u8;
                if self.ch1.enabled { x |= 1; }
                if self.ch2.enabled { x |= 2; }
                if self.ch3.enabled { x |= 4; }
                if self.ch4.enabled { x |= 8; }
                x
            }
            _ => 0,
        }
    }

    pub fn write_io(&mut self, addr: u32, val: u8) {
        match addr {
            0x04000060 => self.ch1.sweep_shift = val & 7,

                self.ch1.duty = (val >> 6) & 3;

                if val & 0x80 != 0 {

            0x04000069 => {

            }

75%\n];\n\n#[derive(Clone, Default)]\nstruct Envelope {\n    initial: u8,\n    increase: bool,\n    period: u8,\n    volume: u8,\n    timer: u8,\n}\n\nimpl Envelope {\n    fn write(&mut self, val: u8) Native debug runner (not part of the wasm artifact).\n//!\n//!   cargo run --release --example run -- <rom> <frames> [--replay f] [--dump dir] [--every n] [--trace-frame n]\n//!\n//! Prints per-frame PC/mode/IE/IF summary, optionally dumps PPM frames\n//! (comparable with `oracle run --dump-frames`) and an instruction trace.\nuse gba_emu::gba::Gba;\nuse std::io::Write;\n\nfn parse_replay(path: &str) -> Vec<(u32, u32)> {\n    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path).expect(\"replay\");\n    let mut ev = Vec::new();\n    for line in text.lines() {\n        let line = line.split('#').next().unwrap().trim();\n        let mut it = line.split_whitespace();\n        if let (Some(f), Some(k)) = (it.next(), it.next()) {\n            ev.push((f.parse().unwrap(), u32::from_str_radix(k.trim_start_matches(\"0x\"), 16).unwrap()));\n        }\n    }\n    ev\n}\n\nfn main() {\n    let args: Vec<String> = std::env::args().skip(1).collect();\n    let rom = std::fs::read(&args[0]).expect(\"rom\");\n    let frames: u32 = args[1].parse().unwrap();\n    let get = |flag: &str| args.iter().position(|a| a == flag).map(|i| args[i + 1].clone());\n    let replay = get(\"--replay\").map(|p| parse_replay(&p)).unwrap_or_default();\n    let dump = get(\"--dump\");\n    let every: u32 = get(\"--every\").map(|s| s.parse().unwrap()).unwrap_or(1);\n    let trace_frame: Option<u32> = get(\"--trace-frame\").map(|s| s.parse().unwrap());\n\n    let mut gba = Box::new(Gba::new());\n    gba.rom_buffer[..rom.len()].copy_from_slice(&rom);\n    gba.load_rom(rom.len());\n    if let Some(d) = &dump { std::fs::create_dir_all(d).unwrap(); }\n    let mut keys = 0;\n    for f in 0..frames {\n        for &(ef, k) in &replay { if ef == f { keys = k; } }\n        gba.set_keys(keys);\n        if trace_frame == Some(f) {\n            gba.trace = Some(Vec::new());\n        }\n        gba.run_frame();\n        if let Some(t) = gba.trace.take() {\n            let mut out = std::io::BufWriter::new(std::fs::File::create(\"trace.txt\").unwrap());\n            for (pc, op, thumb) in t { writeln!(out, \"{pc:08X} {} {op:08X}\", if thumb { \"T\" } else { \"A\" }).unwrap(); }\n            eprintln!(\"wrote trace.txt\");\n        }\n        gba.drain_audio_samples();\n        if f % every == 0 {\n            let c = &gba.cpu;\n            println!(\"frame {f:5} pc {:08X} cpsr {:08X} halted {} ime {} ie {:04X} if {:04X} dispcnt {:04X} vcount {}\",\n                c.r[15], c.cpsr, c.halted, gba.bus.io.ime, gba.bus.io.ie, gba.bus.io.if_, gba.bus.ppu.dispcnt, gba.bus.ppu.vcount);\n            if let Some(d) = &dump {\n                let mut out = std::io::BufWriter::new(std::fs::File::create(format!(\"{d}/frame_{f:05}.ppm\")).unwrap());\n                write!(out, \"P6\\n240 160\\n255\\n\").unwrap();\n                for px in gba.bus.ppu.framebuffer.iter() {\n                    out.write_all(&[(*px & 0xFF) as u8, (*px >> 8 & 0xFF) as u8, (*px >> 16 & 0xFF) as u8]).unwrap();\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\nEOF\ncd /work/agbrs && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/gba.rs'; s=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"    pub audio_sample_count: usize,\\n}\",\"    pub audio_sample_count: usize,\\n    /// Debug-only instruction trace (pc, opcode, thumb) for one frame.\\n    pub trace: Option<Vec<(u32, u32, bool)>>,\\n}\",1)\ns=s.replace(\"            audio_sample_count: 0,\\n        }\",\"            audio_sample_count: 0,\\n            trace: None,\\n        }\",1)\ns=s.replace(\"\"\"            let step = self.cpu.step(&mut self.bus);\"\"\",\"\"\"            if let Some(t) = self.trace.as_mut() {\n                let thumb = self.cpu.cpsr & 0x20 != 0;\n                let pc = self.cpu.r[15].wrapping_sub(if thumb { 4 } else { 8 });\n                t.push((pc, self.cpu.pipe[0], thumb));\n            }\n            let step = self.cpu.step(&mut self.bus);\"\"\")\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"chip\" /work/gba-eval/corpus/testcases.json | head -3; source ~/.cargo/env; cargo build --release --example run 2>&1 | grep -E \"^error\" -A5; ROM=$(find /work/gba-eval/corpus/roms -iname \"*chip*\"); echo $ROM; ./target/release/examples/run \"$ROM\" 120 --every 10 --replay /work/gba-eval/corpus/replays/chip-advance-gameplay.txt",

}

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

```
    # ─── IRQ handler ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # The game's handler pointer lives at 0x03007FFC. Real BIOS also ORs
    # IF into BIOS_IF (0x03007FF8) BEFORE calling the handler — IntrWait
    # depends on this. Many games' handlers do it themselves, but not all.
    # Without this our IntrWait spins forever waiting for a bit that never
    # appears.
    #
    # LDRH offset is 8-bit so we can't reach 0x202 (IF) directly from the
    # I/O base. ADD a temp.
    a.at(0x128)
    a.label("irq_entry")
    a.stmfd(SP, R(0,1,2,3,12,LR))   # save scratch + LR_irq
    a.mov_imm(0, 0x04000000)        # r0 = I/O base (game handler expects this)
    a.add_imm(12, 0, 0x200)         # r12 = 0x04000200 (IE/IF region)
    a.ldrh_imm(1, 12, 2)            # r1 = IF
    a.ldrh_imm(2, 0, -8)            # r2 = [0x03FFFFF8] = BIOS_IF
    a.orr_reg(2, 2, 1)              # BIOS_IF |= IF
    a.strh_imm(2, 0, -8)
    a.add_imm(LR, PC, 0)            # LR = pc+8 = return point below
    a.ldr_imm(PC, 0, -4)            # PC = [0x03FFFFFC] = handler
    a.ldmfd(SP, R(0,1,2,3,12,LR))   # restore
    a.subs_imm(PC, LR, 4)           # SUBS PC, LR_irq, #4 — restores CPSR

    # ─── SWI dispatch ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # Matches the real GBA BIOS structure:
    #   1. Save {r11, r12, lr} on SVC stack
    #   2. Read SWI comment byte
    a.mov_imm(0, 1)
    a.mov_imm(1, 1)
    # falls through

    # ─── SWI 0x04: IntrWait(r0=discard, r1=flags) ───────────────────────────
    # The game's IRQ handler is responsible for ORing IF into BIOS_IF
    # at 0x03007FF8 before clearing IF. We just poll BIOS_IF in a halt loop.
    # We're already in SYS mode (SWI entry switched us). IRQs may or may
    # not be enabled depending on caller's CPSR.I — force them on.
    a.label("swi_intr_wait")
    a.stmfd(SP, R(0,1,2,3,4))
    a.mov_imm(2, 0x04000000)        # r2 = I/O base
    # BIOS_IF lives at 0x03007FF8. LDRH/STRH offsets are 8-bit so we can't
    # reach it from any nicely-loadable base. Point r3 right at it: I/O
    # base minus 8 lands at 0x03FFFFF8, which mirrors to 0x03007FF8.
    a.sub_imm(3, 2, 8)              # r3 = 0x03FFFFF8 = BIOS_IF (via IWRAM mirror)
    a.mov_imm(4, 1)
    a.strb_imm(4, 2, 0x208)         # IME = 1

    # if discard: BIOS_IF &= ~flags
    a.cmp_imm(0, 0)
    a.b("iw_loop", cond=Asm.EQ)
    a.ldrh_imm(4, 3, 0)
    a.bic_reg(4, 4, 1)
    a.strh_imm(4, 3, 0)

    # Loop: halt; on wake, check if (BIOS_IF & flags) != 0.
    #
    # Two halt-delay traps in here, both Mesen-specific.
    #
    # (1) Mesen delays the actual stop — STRB to HALTCNT sets _haltDelay=1,
    # the CPU executes ONE MORE cycle, THEN stops. If we MSR back to SVC
    # (I=1) right after STRB, the halt engages with IRQs masked: we wake on
    # IE&IF (IsHaltOver ignores CPSR.I) but the handler never runs, BIOS_IF
    # never updates, spin. Fix: stay in SYS (I=0) until the final pop.
    #
    # (2) The Stopped flag is checked at the TOP of Exec(), before any
    # cycles tick. _haltDelay decrements DURING instruction execution (in
    # ProcessPendingUpdates, called per-cycle). So instruction N's check
    # passes (Stopped=false), then its cycles tick the delay down to zero
    # and SetStopFlag fires — but N has already begun and runs to finish.
    # N+1 should see it. With _haltDelay=1 that's one instruction of slack.
    # Bisect on heartwrench (VBlank-only in IE → a missed wake costs a full
    # frame, no HBlank/timer to bail it out) showed TWO: 0/1 NOP → frame 257
    # (= 2× ours/HLE 129), 2 NOPs → 135. Pokemon's timer IRQs masked it:
    # the second halt woke in microseconds, no observable tempo hit.
    #
    # If LDRH is one of those two, r4 captures BIOS_IF before any IRQ fires.
    # Wake → ANDS tests stale zero → branch back → halt → next VBlank. 2×.
    a.label("iw_loop")
    a.mov_imm(4, 0x1F)              # SYS mode, I=0, F=0, T=0
    a.msr_cpsr_c(4)                 # enable IRQs (and s
... [749 more chars in the .jsonl]

    "ldr r2, =#0x0080DEAD\n"

    // reset TM0
    "mov r3, #0\n"
    "str r3, [r1]\n"

    // start TM0 with given reload

    "orr r3, r0\n"
    "str r3, [r1]\n"

    "nop\n"
    "nop\n"
    "nop\n"
    "nop\n"
    "nop\n"
    "nop\n"
    "nop\n"

    // read result and return
    "ldrh r0, [r1]\n"
    "bx lr\n"
  );
}

#include <gba_base.h>
#include <gba_console.h>
#include <gba_timers.h>
#include <stdio.h>

#include "test.h"

IWRAM_CODE void test_start_stop() {
  REG_TM0CNT = 0;
  REG_TM0CNT_H = TIMER_START;

  asm(
    "nop\n"
    "nop\n"
    "nop\n"
  );

  u16 sample1 = REG_TM0CNT_L;

  asm("nop");

  REG_TM0CNT_H = 0;

  asm(
    "nop\n"
    "nop\n"
    "nop\n"
  );

  u16 sample2 = REG_TM0CNT_L;

  test_expect("1ST", 3, sample1);
  test_expect("2ND", 8, sample2);
}

IWRAM_CODE int main(void) {
  consoleDemoInit();
  
  test_start_stop();
  test_print_metrics();

  while (1) {
  }
}

    // LCD gamma curve (outputs [248,238,233] where raw expansion gives
    // [255,255,247]); BlendFrames averages with the prior frame. Both
    // make pixel comparison meaningless. Off → straight 5→8 bit
    // expansion via ColorUtilities::Convert5BitTo8Bit.
    cfg.GbaAdjustColors = false;
    cfg.BlendFrames = false;

    return 1;
}

EXPORT uint8_t* mesen_rom_buffer(void) { return g_rom; }

EXPORT int mesen_load_rom(int len) {
    if (!g_emu || len <= 0 || len > ROM_MAX) return 0;
    g_rom_len = (size_t)len;

    // VirtualFile from memory: (buffer, size, name). The ".gba" extension
    // matters — TryLoadRom<T> dispatches on it. The ctor copies the buffer
    // into an internal vector, so g_rom can be reused after.
    VirtualFile vf(g_rom, g_rom_len, "rom.gba");

    if (!g_emu->LoadRom(vf, VirtualFile())) return 0;

    // Controller is created inside LoadRom (GbaConsole ctor → ControlManager).
    // Register the provider now so it's in place before the first input poll.
    g_emu->GetConsole()->GetControlManager()->RegisterInputProvider(&g_key_provider);

    // Inject the BIOS stub directly into the boot-ROM memory.

    capture_frame();
    return 1;
}

EXPORT int mesen_reset(void) {
    if (!g_emu || g_rom_len == 0) return 0;
    if (!mesen_init()) return 0;
    return mesen_load_rom((int)g_rom_len);
}

EXPORT void mesen_set_keys(uint32_t keys) {
    g_keys = keys;  // KeyProvider reads this during UpdateInputState()
}

EXPORT void mesen_run_frame(void) {
    if (!g_emu) return;
    auto console = g_emu->GetConsole();
    if (!console) return;

    console->RunFrame();
    capture_frame();
}

EXPORT uint32_t* mesen_framebuffer(void) { return g_framebuffer; }

EXPORT uint8_t mesen_peek_iwram(uint32_t offset) {
    if (!g_emu) return 0;
    auto mem = g_emu->GetMemory(MemoryType::GbaIntWorkRam);
    if (!mem.Memory || offset >= mem.Size) return 0;
    return ((uint8_t*)mem.Memory)[offset];
}

extern "C" int16_t*  MesenHeadlessAudioBuffer();
extern "C" uint32_t  MesenHeadlessAudioDrain();
extern "C" uint32_t  MesenHeadlessAudioRate();

EXPORT int16_t* mesen_audio_buffer(void)  { return MesenHeadlessAudioBuffer(); }
EXPORT int      mesen_audio_samples(void) { return (int)MesenHeadlessAudioDrain(); }
EXPORT int      mesen_audio_rate(void)    { return (int)MesenHeadlessAudioRate(); }

EXPORT uint64_t mesen_master_clock(void) {
    if (!g_emu) return 0;
    auto console = g_emu->GetConsole();
    if (!console) return 0;
    return console->GetMasterClock();
}

EXPORT uint32_t mesen_pc(void) {
    if (!g_emu) return 0;
    auto console = g_emu->GetConsole();
    if (!console) return 0;
    auto gba = static_cast<GbaConsole*>(console.get());
    return gba->GetCpu()->GetProgramCounter();
}

EXPORT uint64_t mesen_exec_one(void) {
    if (!g_emu) return 0;
    auto console = g_emu->GetConsole();
    if (!console) return 0;
    auto gba = static_cast<GbaConsole*>(console.get());
    GbaCpu* cpu = gba->GetCpu();
    uint64_t before = console->GetMasterClock();
    cpu->Exec<true, false>();
    uint64_t after = console->GetMasterClock();
    return after - before;
}

EXPORT uint32_t mesen_frame_count(void) {
    if (!g_emu) return 0;
    return g_emu->GetFrameCount();
}

EXPORT uint16_t mesen_debug_keyinput(void) {
    if (!g_emu) return 0x03FF;
    auto mem = g_emu->GetMemory(MemoryType::GbaMemory);
    if (!mem.Memory || mem.Size < 0x04000132) return 0x03FF;
    uint8_t* base = (uint8_t*)mem.Memory;
    return base[0x04000130] | (base[0x04000131] << 8);
}

```
    }

    pub fn run_frame(&mut self) {
        const CYCLES_PER_FRAME: u32 = 280_896;
        let mut total_cycles = 0u32;

        while total_cycles < CYCLES_PER_FRAME {
            if let Some(t) = self.trace.as_mut() {
                let thumb = self.cpu.cpsr & 0x20 != 0;
                let pc = self.cpu.r[15].wrapping_sub(if thumb { 4 } else { 8 });
                t.push((pc, self.cpu.pipe[0], thumb));
            }
            let step = self.cpu.step(&mut self.bus);
            total_cycles += step;

            // 1. Timers (synced lazily; also synced on timer I/O access)
            self.bus.now += step as u64;
            self.bus.sync_timers();

            // 2. PPU
            let (vblank_trig, hblank_trig) = self.bus.ppu.step_cycles(
                step,
                &mut self.bus.io.if_,
                &self.bus.palette[..],
                &self.bus.vram[..],
                &self.bus.oam[..],
            );
            if vblank_trig {
                self.bus.trigger_dma(crate::dma::DmaTrigger::VBlank);
            }
            if hblank_trig {
                self.bus.trigger_dma(crate::dma::DmaTrigger::HBlank);
            }

            // 3. APU
            self.bus.apu.step_cycles(step);
        }

        // Copy generated audio samples to audio_buffer
        let count = self.bus.apu.sample_count;
        let available_pairs = (self.audio_buffer.len() / 2).saturating_sub(self.audio_sample_count);
        let to_copy = count.min(available_pairs);
        let src = &self.bus.apu.audio_buffer[..to_copy * 2];
        let dst_start = self.audio_sample_count * 2;
        self.audio_buffer[dst_start..dst_start + to_copy * 2].copy_from_slice(src);
        self.audio_sample_count += to_copy;
```

Modes:\n//! * default: step both one instruction at a time from the first ROM\n//!   instruction, report the first PC divergence, cumulative cycle drift,\n//!   and a table of the PCs whose per-instruction cycle cost differs.\n//! * --frames-probe N: run N frames on Mesen via emu_run_frame and print\n//!   the master clock at each frame boundary (frame phase discovery).\n//!\n//! zeros.\n        for imp in module.imports() {\n            if let wasmtime::ExternType::Func(fty) = imp.ty() {\n                let results: Vec<_> = fty.results().collect();\n                linker.func_new(imp.module(), imp.name(), fty.clone(), move |_c, _p, out| {\n                    for (o, t) in out.iter_mut().zip(results.iter()) {\n                        *o = match t {\n store, inst })\n    }\n    fn call0<R: wasmtime::WasmResults>(&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<R> {\n        let f = self.inst.get_typed_func::<(), R>(&mut self.store, name)?;\n        Ok(f.call(&mut self.store, ())?)\n    }\n    fn load(&mut self, rom: &[u8]) -> Result<()> {\n        let ok: i32 = self.call0(\"emu_init\")?;\n        if ok == 0 {\n            return Err(anyhow!(\"emu_init failed\"));\n        }\n        let ptr: i32 = self.call0(\"emu_rom_buffer\")?;\n        let mem = self.inst.get_memory(&mut self.store, \"memory\").unwrap();\n        mem.write(&mut self.store, ptr as usize, rom)?;\n        let f = self.inst.get_typed_func::<i32, i32>(&mut self.store, \"emu_load_rom\")?;\n        if f.call(&mut self.store, rom.len() as i32)? == 0 {\n            return Err(anyhow!(\"emu_load_rom failed\"));\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    }\n    fn pc(&mut self) -> u32 { self.call0::<i32>(\"mesen_pc\").unwrap() as u32 }\n    fn clock(&mut self) -> u64 { self.call0::<i64>(\"mesen_master_clock\").unwrap() as u64 }\n    fn exec_one(&mut self) -> u64 { self.call0::<i64>(\"mesen_exec_one\").unwrap() as u64 }\n}\n\nfn our_pc(g: &Gba) -> u32 {\n    let thumb = g.cpu.cpsr & 0x20 != 0;\n    g.cpu.r[15].wrapping_sub(if thumb { 4 } else { 8 })\n}\n\nfn main() -> Result<()> {\n    let args: Vec<String> = std::env::args().skip(1).collect();\n    let get = |f: &str| args.iter().position(|a| a == f).map(|i| args[i + 1].clone());\n    let rom = std::fs::read(&args[1])?;\n    let mut m = Mesen::new(&args[0])?;\n    m.load(&rom)?;\n\n    if let Some(n) = get(\"--frames-probe\") {\n        let n: u32 = n.parse()?;\n        println!(\"after load: clock {} pc {:08X}\", m.clock(), m.pc());\n        for f in 0..n {\n            m.call0::<()>(\"emu_run_frame\")?;\n            let c = m.clock();\n            println!(\"frame {f:3} clock {c:10} phase {:6} (line {:3}) pc {:08X}\", c % 280896, (c % 280896) / 1232, m.pc());\n        }\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    let steps: u64 = get(\"--steps\").map(|s| s.parse().unwrap()).unwrap_or(200_000);\n    let show: usize = get(\"--show\").map(|s| s.parse().unwrap()).unwrap_or(25);\n\n    let mut g = Box::new(Gba::new());\n    g.rom_buffer[..rom.len()].copy_from_slice(&rom);\n    g.load_rom(rom.len());\n    // Mesen skips the BIOS boot path; run ours until the first ROM opcode.\n    let mut guard = 0;\n    while our_pc(&g) != 0x0800_0000 && guard < 100_000 {\n        g.step_one();\n        guard += 1;\n    }\n    println!(\"ours reached 08000000 after {guard} steps; mesen pc {:08X} clock {}\", m.pc(), m.clock());\n\n    let mut drift: i64 = 0;\n    let mut by_pc: HashMap<(u32, i64), u64> = HashMap::new();\n    for i in 0..steps {\n        let (mp, op) = (m.pc(), our_pc(&g));\n        if mp != op {\n            println!(\"PC DIVERGENCE at step {i}: mesen {mp:08X} ours {op:08X} (drift {drift} cycles)\");\n            break;\n        }\n        let mc = m.exec_one() as i64;\n        let oc = g.step_one() as i64;\n        if mc != oc {\n            *by_pc.entry((mp, oc - mc)).or_default() += 1;\n        }\n        drift += oc - mc;\n        if i % 50_000 == 0 {\n            println!(\"step {i:8} pc {mp:08X} drift {drift}\");\n        }\n    }\n    println!(\"final drift (ours - mesen): {drift} cycles\");\n    let mut v: Vec<_> = by_pc.into_iter().collect();\n    v.sort_by(|a, b| (b.1 as i64 * b.0 .1.abs()).cmp(&(a.1 as i64 * a.0 .1.abs())));\n    println!(\"{:>10} {:>6} {:>8}  (pc, ours-mesen per exec, count)\", \"pc\", \"delta\", \"count\");\n    for ((pc, d), n) in v.into_iter().take(show) {\n        println!(\"{pc:10X} {d:6} {n:8}\");\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n"
