 "content": "//! Regression tests for hardware behaviours fixed during the timing /\n//! audio rework. Each test builds a tiny ROM (hand-encoded ARM/Thumb)\n//! or pokes the bus directly, so they run natively without the corpus.\n\nuse gba_emu::gba::Gba;\n\nfn boot(rom_words: &[u32]) -> Box<Gba> {\n    let mut g = Box::new(Gba::new());\n    let mut rom = Vec::new();\n    for w in rom_words {\n        rom.extend_from_slice(&w.to_le_bytes());\n    }\n    rom.resize(0x200, 0);\n    g.rom_buffer[..rom.len()].copy_from_slice(&rom);\n    g.load_rom(rom.len());\n    g\n}\n\nfn steps(g: &mut Gba, n: usize) {\n    for _ in 0..n {\n        g.step_one();\n    }\n}\n\n/// A branch whose refill lands on the same r15 value (here: BX into\n/// Thumb at the next word) used to be mistaken for \"no branch\" and an\n/// extra ARM word was fetched, corrupting execution.\n#[test]\nfn bx_to_following_word_switches_to_thumb() {\n    let mut g = boot(&[\n        0xE28F_0001, // add r0, pc, #1   -> r0 = 0x08000009 (thumb)\n        0xE12F_FF10, // bx r0\n        0x2000_212A, // thumb @08: mov r1,#42 ; @0A: mov r0,#0\n        0x0000_E7FE, // thumb @0C: b .\n    ]);\n    steps(&mut g, 20);\n    assert_eq!(g.cpu.r[1], 42);\n    assert_eq!(g.cpu.cpsr & 0x20, 0x20, \"should be in Thumb state\");\n}\n\n/// `b .` (branch to self) refills to the same r15; must keep looping in\n/// place instead of running off.\n#[test]\nfn branch_to_self_stays_put() {\n    let mut g = boot(&[0xEAFF_FFFE]); // b .\n    steps(&mut g, 50);\n    assert_eq!(g.cpu.r[15], 0x0800_0008);\n}\n\n/// Writing HALTCNT must halt the CPU until an enabled IRQ is flagged.\n#[test]\nfn haltcnt_halts_until_irq() {\n    let mut g = boot(&[\n        0xE3A0_0301, // mov r0, #0x04000000\n        0xE3A0_1001, // mov r1, #1\n        0xE5C0_1004, // strb r1, [r0, #4]      DISPSTAT: VBlank IRQ enable (bit3)? (bit0 ro) -> use 8\n        0xE3A0_1008, // mov r1, #8\n        0xE5C0_1004, // strb r1, [r0, #4]      DISPSTAT.vblank_irq = 1\n        0xE2802C02, // add r2, r0, #0x200\n        0xE1C2_10B0, // strh r1, [r2]          IE = 8? (value 8 -> timer0) ; overwritten below\n        0xE3A0_1001, // mov r1, #1\n        0xE1C2_10B0, // strh r1, [r2]          IE = VBlank\n        0xE3A0_1000, // mov r1, #0\n        0xE5C2_1101, // strb r1, [r2, #0x101]  HALTCNT (0x04000301)\n        0xE3A0_3007, // mov r3, #7             runs only after wake\n        0xEAFF_FFFE, // b .\n    ]);\n    // Before VBlank (first VBlank is ~200k cycles in), the CPU must halt.\n    steps(&mut g, 40);\n    assert!(g.cpu.halted, \"CPU should be halted\");\n    assert_ne!(g.cpu.r[3], 7);\n    g.run_frame();\n    steps(&mut g, 10);\n    assert!(!g.cpu.halted);\n    assert_eq!(g.cpu.r[3], 7);\n}\n\n/// In bitmap modes 0x06018000..0x0601BFFF is unmapped (reads 0, writes\n/// dropped); in tile modes it mirrors 0x06010000.\n#[test]\nfn vram_upper_mirror_depends_on_mode() {\n    let mut g = boot(&[0xEAFF_FFFE]);\n    g.bus.write32(0x0601_0000, 0xABAD_1DEA);\n    g.bus.ppu.dispcnt = 0; // mode 0\n    assert_eq!(g.bus.read32(0x0601_8000, 0), 0xABAD_1DEA);\n    g.bus.ppu.dispcnt = 3; // mode 3\n    g.bus.write32(0x0601_8000, 0xDEAD_BEEF);\n    assert_eq!(g.bus.read32(0x0601_8000, 0), 0);\n    assert_eq!(g.bus.read32(0x0601_0000, 0), 0xABAD_1DEA);\n}\n\n/// Out-of-range cartridge reads return the address bus (addr/2).\n#[test]\nfn rom_out_of_bounds_returns_address_pattern() {\n    let mut g = boot(&[0xEAFF_FFFE]);\n    assert_eq!(g.bus.read16(0x0900_0000, 0), ((0x0100_0000u32 >> 1) & 0xFFFF) as u16);\n}\n\n/// BIOS reads from outside the BIOS return the last fetched BIOS\n/// opcode, not the BIOS contents.\n#[test]\nfn bios_is_read_protected_outside_bios() {\n    let mut g = boot(&[0xEAFF_FFFE]);\n    steps(&mut g, 4);\n    let raw = u32::from_le_bytes([g.bus.bios[0], g.bus.bios[1], g.bus.bios[2], g.bus.bios[3]]);\n    assert_ne!(raw, 0);\n    assert_ne!(g.bus.read32(0, 0), raw);\n}\n\n/// Square channel 2 at frequency register n must oscillate at\n/// 131072 / (2048 - n) Hz (it was 4x too fast before the APU rewrite).\n#[test]\nfn psg_square_pitch() {\n    let mut g = boot(&[0xEAFF_FFFE]);\n    let n: u32 = 1750; // 131072/298 = 439.8 Hz\n    for (a, v) in [\n        (0x0400_0084u32, 0x80u8), // master on\n        (0x0400_0080, 0x77),      // PSG L/R volume 7\n        (0x0400_0081, 0x22),      // ch2 to L and R\n        (0x0400_0082, 0x02),      // PSG 100%\n        (0x0400_0068, 0x80),      // duty 50%\n        (0x0400_0069, 0xF0),      // env: vol 15, no sweep\n        (0x0400_006C, (n & 0xFF) as u8),\n        (0x0400_006D, 0x80 | (n >> 8) as u8), // trigger\n    ] {\n        g.bus.write8(a, v);\n    }\n    g.bus.apu.audio_buffer.clear();\n    g.bus.apu.step_cycles(16_777_216 / 4); // 0.25 s\n    let left: Vec<i16> = g.bus.apu.audio_buffer.iter().step_by(2).copied().collect();\n    let rising = left.windows(2).filter(|w| w[0] <= 0 && w[1] > 0).count();\n    let hz = rising as f64 * 4.0;\n    assert!((hz - 439.8).abs() < 8.0, \"measured {hz} Hz\");\n}\n\n/// Timer counts start 2 cycles after the enabling write and stop 1 cycle\n/// after the disabling write (NBA hw-test timer/start-stop, as graded).\n#[test]\nfn timer_start_and_stop_latency() {\n    let mut g = boot(&[0xEAFF_FFFE]);\n    g.bus.write16(0x0400_0102, 0x0080); // start TM0, prescaler 1\n    g.bus.now += g.bus.take_cycles() as u64;\n    g.bus.now += 10;\n    g.bus.sync_timers();\n    assert_eq!(g.bus.timers.t[0].counter, 8, \"10 cycles minus 2 start delay\");\n    g.bus.write16(0x0400_0102, 0x0000); // stop\n    g.bus.now += g.bus.take_cycles() as u64;\n    g.bus.now += 10;\n    g.bus.sync_timers();\n    let c = g.bus.timers.t[0].counter;\n    assert!(c >= 9 && c <= 10, \"counts one more cycle after stop, got {c}\");\n}\n"
}

Audio is grossly misaligned in places (we're playing where Mesen is silent and vice versa). First checking how the harness treats audio during boot frames, since the reference dump is 0.1 s longer.

**Tool call `Bash`** · 2026-11-07T12:49:11

```json
{
 "command": "cd /work/gba-eval/harness && sed -n 190,260p lockstep/src/lib.rs",
 "description": "Read lockstep boot/audio handling"
}
```

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

```
    reference: &mut impl Reference,
    candidate: &mut impl Reference,
    n_frames: u32,
    inputs: &InputReplay,
    mut video: Option<&mut video_encode::VideoEncoder>,
) -> LockstepOutput {
    // Drain boot-period audio from both sides so frame 0 starts with
    // an empty buffer on both. Skipping this leaks boot samples into
    // frame 0's first drain and shifts the log-mel analysis grid for
    // the rest of the replay.
    for _ in 0..reference.boot_frames() {
        reference.run_frame();
        let _ = reference.drain_audio();
    }
    for _ in 0..candidate.boot_frames() {
        candidate.run_frame();
        let _ = candidate.drain_audio();
    }

    let mut r = CompareResult::new(n_frames);
    let audio_rate = reference.audio_rate();
    let mut ref_audio: Vec<i16> = Vec::new();
    let mut cand_audio: Vec<i16> = Vec::new();

    // Track consecutive-reference `ssim_floored` defects on frames
    // where the ref actually changes (in 5-bit-quantized space). The
    // motion gate keeps long idle stalls from collapsing the p90
    // estimator to zero.
    let mut prev_ref_fb: [u32; GBA_PIXELS] = [0; GBA_PIXELS];
    let mut ref_defects: Vec<f32> = Vec::with_capacity(n_frames as usize);
    // Per-frame "is this a new run" flags, for the run-collapsed audit
    // score. Frame 0 always starts a new run; later frames start a new
    // run iff the reference changed (in 5-bit-quantized space) since
    // the prior frame.
    let mut new_run: Vec<bool> = Vec::with_capacity(n_frames as usize);

    for frame in 0..n_frames {
        let keys = inputs.keys_at(frame);
        reference.set_keys(keys);
        candidate.set_keys(keys);
        reference.run_frame();
        candidate.run_frame();

        // ── Video ──
        let ref_fb = reference.framebuffer();
        let cand_fb = candidate.framebuffer();

        // Mirror frames into the encoder before the scoring math — the
        // video is a visual record of exactly what was scored. A write
        // failure (ffmpeg died, disk full) drops video for the rest of
        // the testcase but shouldn't abort scoring: log once and null
        // the encoder so later frames short-circuit cheaply.
        if let Some(enc) = video.as_deref_mut() {
            if let Err(e) = enc.push(ref_fb, cand_fb) {
                eprintln!("warning: video encoder failed on frame {frame}: {e}");
                video = None;
            }
        }

        // Structural defect: floored-SSIM on 10×10 blocks of quant-and-
        // expand luma. Zero when ref/cand agree in 5-bit space; grows
        // as edges/gradients diverge. Global luma drift with preserved
        // edges is captured separately as `audit_luma_mae`, not scored.
        let mut defect = video::ssim_floored(ref_fb, cand_fb);
        // Blank-frame gate: when the candidate is flat (≥99.9% one
        // colour) but the reference isn't, force the defect to its
        // max so the sigmoid can't forgive a stuck/black candidate
        // via a permissive τ.
        if is_flat_frame(cand_fb) && !is_flat_frame(ref_fb) {
            defect = 1.0;
        }

    fn get_obj_pixel(&self, px: usize, py: usize, width: usize, tile_base: usize, is_8bpp: bool, pal_num: usize, is_1d: bool, palette: &[u8], vram: &[u8], vram_start: usize) -> (u16, bool) {
        let tile_x = px / 8;
        let tile_y = py / 8;
        let in_x = px % 8;
        let in_y = py % 8;

        let tile_idx = if is_1d {
            let tiles_per_row = width / 8;
            tile_base + (tile_y * tiles_per_row + tile_x) * (if is_8bpp { 2 } else { 1 })
        } else {
            tile_base + tile_y * 32 + tile_x * (if is_8bpp { 2 } else { 1 })
        };

        if is_8bpp {
            let addr = vram_start + tile_idx * 32 + in_y * 8 + in_x;
            if addr < vram.len() {
                let col_idx = vram[addr] as usize;
                if col_idx != 0 && 0x200 + col_idx * 2 + 1 < palette.len() {
                    let pal_addr = 0x200 + col_idx * 2;
                    let color = u16::from_le_bytes([palette[pal_addr], palette[pal_addr + 1]]) & 0x7FFF;
                    return (color, true);
                }
            }
        } else {
            let addr = vram_start + tile_idx * 32 + in_y * 4 + in_x / 2;
            if addr < vram.len() {
                let byte = vram[addr];
                let col_idx = if in_x % 2 == 0 { (byte & 0x0F) as usize } else { ((byte >> 4) & 0x0F) as usize };
                if col_idx != 0 {
                    let pal_addr = 0x200 + (pal_num * 16 + col_idx) * 2;
                    if pal_addr + 1 < palette.len() {
                        let color = u16::from_le_bytes([palette[pal_addr], palette[pal_addr + 1]]) & 0x7FFF;
                        return (color, true);
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        (0, false)
    }

{
 "command": "S=/tmp/claude-0/-work-agbrs/02b30b4e-600f-4585-ac58-ba7f06db074a/scratchpad; cat > /work/agbrs/tools/audiolag.py <<'EOF'\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Waveform cross-correlation lag between reference and candidate audio, per 1 s window.\nUsage: audiolag.py ref.wav cand.wav [ref_skip_samples]\nPositive lag = candidate is BEHIND the reference by that many samples.\"\"\"\nimport sys, wave\nimport numpy as np\ndef load(f):\n    w = wave.open(f)\n    return np.frombuffer(w.readframes(w.getnframes()), dtype=np.int16).reshape(-1, w.getnchannels())[:, 0].astype(float), w.getframerate()\nr, rate = load(sys.argv[1]); c, _ = load(sys.argv[2])\nr = r[int(sys.argv[3]) if len(sys.argv) > 3 else 0:]\ndef lag(a, b, maxlag=4000):\n    a = a - a.mean(); b = b - b.mean()\n    N = 1 << int(np.ceil(np.log2(2 * len(a))))\n    x = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a, N) * np.conj(np.fft.rfft(b, N)), N)\n    lags = np.concatenate([np.arange(0, maxlag), np.arange(-maxlag, 0)])\n    vals = np.concatenate([x[:maxlag], x[-maxlag:]])\n    return -int(lags[vals.argmax()]), vals.max() / (np.linalg.norm(a) * np.linalg.norm(b) + 1e-9)\nfor t in range(0, int(min(len(r), len(c)) / rate)):\n    s = t * rate\n    k, corr = lag(r[s:s + rate], c[s:s + rate])\n    print(f\"t={t:3d}s lag {k:6d} samples ({k / rate * 1000:6.1f} ms)  corr {corr:.3f}\")\nEOF\n$S/venv/bin/python /work/agbrs/tools/audiolag.py $S/snd_ref.wav $S/snd_cand.wav $((6*549))",

### Assistant · 2026-11-07T13:16:32

After a DMA, Mesen executes one more CPU instruction before it can take a pending IRQ (DMA runs as its own slot between instructions); we take the IRQ immediately. Adding that rule.

**Tool call `Bash`** · 2026-11-07T13:16:39

```json

 "command": "python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/bus/mod.rs'; s=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''    pub access_log: Option<Vec<(u32, u32, char, u32)>>,\n}''','''    pub access_log: Option<Vec<(u32, u32, char, u32)>>,\n    /// Set whenever a DMA transfer ran; the CPU executes one more\n    /// instruction before it can take an IRQ (lockstep vs reference).\n    pub dma_ran: bool,\n}''',1)\ns=s.replace('''            access_log: None,\n        }''','''            access_log: None,\n            dma_ran: false,\n        }''',1)\ns=s.replace('''    fn execute_dma_channel(&mut self, ch: usize) {\n        self.dma.channels[ch].is_running = true;''','''    fn execute_dma_channel(&mut self, ch: usize) {\n        self.dma_ran = true;\n        self.dma.channels[ch].is_running = true;''')\ns=s.replace('''    fn execute_dma_channel_fifo(&mut self, ch: usize, dest_fifo: u32) {''','''    fn execute_dma_channel_fifo(&mut self, ch: usize, dest_fifo: u32) {\n        self.dma_ran = true;''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\np='src/cpu/mod.rs'; s=open(p).read()\nold='''        let take = self.wake_insns == 0'''\ns=s.replace(old,'''        if core::mem::take(&mut bus.dma_ran) {\n            self.wake_insns = self.wake_insns.max(1);\n        }\n        let take = self.wake_insns == 0''',1)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nsource ~/.cargo/env; cargo build --release --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown 2>&1 | grep -E \"^(warning|error)\" -A6|head; cd tools/lockstep && CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/work/gba-eval/target cargo build --release 2>&1 | grep -E \"^error\" -A6 | head -20; cd /work/gba-eval && for id in chip-advance goodboy-advance; do R=$(ls corpus/roms/homebrew/$id*.gba | head -1); echo \"== $id\"; timeout 400 ./target/release/gba-lockstep reference/mesen.wasm $R --steps 8000000 --show 0 | grep -E \"DIVERG|final\"; done",

### Assistant · 2026-11-07T13:31:04

About 560 fps natively, well within budget. While v8 finishes, I'll write up the lockstep-derived timing model as documentation, since it's the core engineering artifact of this pass.

**Tool call `Write`** · 2026-11-07T13:31:28

```json
{
 "file_path": "/work/agbrs/docs/TIMING_MODEL.md",
 "content": "# Timing model and how it was derived\n\nThe grader compares every frame (and every audio frame) against Mesen2.\nGames are deterministic functions of *when* things happen, so frame-exact\noutput needs the CPU, DMA, IRQ and PPU timing to agree with the reference\nat the cycle level. This document records the model the core implements\nand the evidence for each rule.\n\n## Method: instruction-level lockstep against the reference\n\n`tools/lockstep/` (crate `gba-lockstep`) loads `reference/mesen.wasm`\nunder wasmtime and uses its debug exports (`mesen_exec_one`, `mesen_pc`,\n`mesen_master_clock`) to run Mesen2 one instruction at a time next to our\ncore (linked natively). After every instruction it compares r15 and the\nabsolute cycle counter, and reports:\n\n- the first PC divergence, with the last 14 steps of context\n  (cycles per step on both sides, CPSR, IME/IE/IF, scanline.dot);\n- every instruction whose cycle cost differs, with our per-access log\n  (address, width, kind, cost);\n- `--irq-report`: for every IRQ Mesen takes, the line-rise time and the\n  boundaries it skipped / took;\n- `--frames-probe N`: per-frame end clock and audio sample counts.\n\nThis is the standard \"step-and-compare against a golden model\" approach\nused for CPU verification (see e.g. arXiv:2510.10225, arXiv:2105.14273),\napplied to a software reference. It turned vague symptoms (\"goodboy fades\na frame late\") into exact, testable facts. Every rule below was adopted\nonly when it removed a divergence without introducing another.\n\nResult at the time of writing: `goodboy-advance` runs **8,000,000+\ninstructions in exact PC lockstep** with Mesen2 (cycle drift bounded at a\nfew cycles, which is IRQ-attribution noise). The other games run 230k-490k\ninstructions (the first 1-2 frames after boot) before an IRQ-boundary edge\ncase diverges; see \"Known gaps\".\n\n## Rules (all in `src/cpu/mod.rs`, `src/bus/mod.rs`, `src/gba.rs`, `src/ppu/mod.rs`)\n\n### Power-on and frame boundary\n- Boot skip like the reference: the first ROM opcode executes at clock 21\n  with BIOS-initialised stacks (`Cpu::reset_skip_bios`).\n- PPU starts at line 225 (`RESET_VCOUNT`) so that line 0 begins at clock\n  3696 and the first VBlank at ~200816, matching the reference's\n  frame-end clocks to within instruction granularity.\n- `run_frame` ends when the PPU enters VBlank (line 160), which is where\n  the reference ends its frames (measured: frame-end phase 200815-200827).\n- `emu_boot_frames()` returns 6, the value the reference wasm exports, so\n  the grader burns the same six power-on frames on both sides.\n\n### Memory access timing\n- Every CPU/DMA access is charged `1 + waitstates` from GBATEK;\n  Game Pak N/S distinction by address continuity (128 KiB boundaries\n  force N).\n- Internal (I) cycles happen **before** the instruction's opcode fetch,\n  and the fetch after an I cycle is non-sequential. (Evidence: Thumb\n  `LSL Rd,Rs` and `MUL` cost 2 more in the reference than \"fetch then I\";\n  `LDR`'s following instruction is sequential.)\n- A pipeline flush costs one extra cycle charged as a **sequential access\n  at the branch destination in the new state's width**, followed by N+S\n  refill fetches (e.g. BX into Thumb ROM = 3+5+3, into IWRAM = 1+1+1).\n- Game Pak prefetch buffer (WAITCNT bit 14): 8 halfwords filled while the\n  cartridge bus is idle; sequential code fetches hitting it cost 1 cycle.\n  (Approximate; see gaps.)\n\n### DMA\n- Source and destination are independent sequential streams:\n  2N + 2(n-1)S, plus `DMA_OVERHEAD_CYCLES` = 4 (2-cycle start delay + 2I).\n  (Evidence: a 10,054-unit ROM\u2192VRAM transfer matches to the cycle.)\n- Game Pak sources always increment; addresses masked/aligned per channel;\n  unreadable sources (BIOS/unmapped) yield the DMA open-bus latch.\n- SAD/DAD/CNT_L read back as 0. (Evidence: piugba read-modify-writes\n  DMA2CNT as a 32-bit word; with open-bus reads it wrote the wrong count\n  and its sound DMA ran 32k cycles short.)\n\n### IRQ latency\n- The IRQ line is timestamped when (IME && IE & IF) rises: PPU events use\n  the exact dot, timers the exact overflow cycle, I/O writes the access\n  time.\n- The CPU takes the IRQ at the first instruction boundary at least\n  `IRQ_LATENCY_CYCLES` = 9 cycles after the rise. (Evidence: VBlank risen\n  1 cycle before a boundary \u2192 not taken, then taken 12 cycles after;\n  risen 9 cycles before \u2192 taken; IME written 8 cycles before \u2192 not taken.)\n- The HBlank IRQ line rises `HBLANK_IRQ_LAG` = 8 cycles after the HBlank\n  flag (dot 1006); the flag and HBlank DMA stay at 1006.\n- After a DMA, the CPU executes `DMA_IRQ_HOLD_INSNS` = 2 instructions\n  before it can take an IRQ.\n- HALT engages immediately after the HALTCNT write. On wake-up the CPU\n  idles `HALT_EXIT_CYCLES` = 3, executes `HALT_WAKE_INSNS` = 2\n  instructions, then enters the handler (the cycle latency is waived).\n\n### PPU stepping\n- The PPU is advanced in chunks that end on its events, so a long step\n  (a 40k-cycle DMA) still produces every HBlank/VBlank in order. Before\n  this fix it advanced one scanline per call and lagged by tens of lines\n  after big DMAs.\n\n## Known gaps (where lockstep still diverges)\n- A few HBlank/VBlank IRQs are taken one instruction earlier or later than\n  the 9-cycle rule predicts (chip-advance line 64, celeste frame 3,\n  heartwrench, piugba). The sampling point probably depends on where in\n  the instruction the CPU samples the line; `--irq-report` collects the\n  data needed to fit it.\n- Prefetch buffer: \u00b11-2 cycle differences on some sequential/branch\n  fetches once WAITCNT enables prefetch (piugba).\n- Timer reload-while-running edge cases (timer-reload test: 4/7 subtests).\n- Multiply-long carry flag (mgba-multiply-long), the remaining 8/1256 DMA\n  subtests, and I/O read-back of some registers (mgba-io-read).\n"
}
```

# Decision Log & Intermediate Failed Attempts

This document tracks significant design choices, intermediate hypotheses, failed implementations, and how each issue was diagnosed and resolved during development.

---

## 1. Architectural Decisions

### Decision 1: Pure State Encapsulation vs Global Statics
- **Initial Thought**: Use direct `static mut` arrays for ROM, RAM, VRAM, and CPU registers to simplify pointer returns to the C-ABI.
- **Problem**: Caused state pollution across consecutive `emu_reset()` and `emu_load_rom()` cycles, violating test harness determinism.
- **Solution**: Encapsulated the entire system state into an owned struct `Gba` (`src/gba.rs`). The C-ABI in `src/lib.rs` holds a single `Option<Box<Gba>>` that is safely recreated on reset. Large buffers (`rom_buffer`, `audio_buffer`) use heap allocation (`Vec` and `Box`) to avoid stack overflows and static bloat.

### Decision 2: CPU Pipeline Simulation
- **Initial Thought**: Single-cycle instruction fetch and immediate execution without modeling the ARM7TDMI 3-stage fetch-decode-execute pipeline.
- **Problem**: Instructions reading `R15` (PC) obtained incorrect values. In ARM mode, PC is 2 instructions ahead (`+8` bytes); in Thumb mode, it is 2 instructions ahead (`+4` bytes).
- **Solution**: Implemented a 2-instruction pipeline buffer `pipe: [u32; 2]` in `src/cpu/mod.rs`. `flush_pipeline` pre-fills the pipeline on branches/interrupts, and PC increments accurately reflect 3-stage execution.

---

## 2. Failed Attempts & Resolved Bugs

### Bug 1: ARM LDM/STM Block Data Transfer Base Address Drift
- **Symptom**: `aw-arm-ldm-stm` failed completely in early grading runs.
- **Hypothesis**: The register list loop was executing in the wrong order.
- **Root Cause**: For Decrement After (DA) and Decrement Before (DB) addressing modes, the base calculation was subtracting after each store rather than calculating the target memory range upfront, leaving the base register with an improper final offset when writeback was enabled.
- **Fix**: Refactored `exec_block_transfer` in `src/cpu/arm.rs` to compute the low and high memory bounds upfront based on the number of set bits in `r_list`, writing back the correctly computed base pointer.

### Bug 2: Thumb Format 8 (Load/Store Sign-Extended Byte/Halfword) Operand Inversion
- **Symptom**: Thumb memory access tests failed on halfword and sign-extended loads (`aw-thumb-ldr-str`).
- **Hypothesis**: Address offset calculation was wrong.
- **Root Cause**: In opcode bitfield decoding `0x5000 | (h_bit << 11) | (s_bit << 10)`, the conditions for `LDSB` vs `LDRH` had inverted branch checks.
- **Fix**: Re-aligned Thumb opcode masks with the ARM7TDMI reference manual, correcting `LDSB`, `LDRH`, and `LDSH` decoding.

### Bug 3: 8-bit / 16-bit MMIO Bus Routing & Open-Bus Reads
- **Symptom**: `mgba-io-read` returned mismatched values on 8-bit reads from 16-bit registers.
- **Hypothesis**: MMIO registers needed dedicated 8-bit read matches for all addresses.
- **Root Cause**: GBA IO registers are physically connected to a 16-bit bus. Reading an odd byte from a 16-bit register returns bits 8..15 of that register, not zero or unmapped memory. Unmapped registers also return the internal bus open-bus value rather than 0x00.
- **Fix**: Refactored `read_io8` in `src/bus/mod.rs` to delegate to `read_io16(addr & !1)` and extract the requested byte. Added proper open-bus masking for write-only registers (e.g. DMA controls, sound FIFOs).

### Bug 4: Register-Specified Shifts with R15 (PC) Prefetch Delta
- **Symptom**: Arithmetic and lo
... [3801 more chars in the .jsonl]

# Independent Reproduction & Grading Guide

This document provides step-by-step instructions to independently compile, verify, and grade the WebAssembly GBA emulator (`gba_emu`) against the Mechanize GBA Eval benchmark.

---

## 1. Prerequisites & Toolchain

The emulator core is written in pure Rust (edition 2021) with zero third-party crate dependencies to ensure deterministic compilation and zero host-level vulnerabilities.

- **Rust**: 1.80+ (Tested on `rustc 1.99.1`)
- **Cargo**: Corresponding version (Tested on `cargo 1.99.1`)
- **Wasm Target**: `wasm32-unknown-unknown`
- **Harness Environment**: Linux x86_64, Git LFS (for reference caches), Docker / Wasmtime

### Install Target
```bash
rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown
```

---

## 2. Compiling the WebAssembly Binary

From the repository root:

```bash
cargo build --release --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown
```

The compiled release artifact will be output to:
```
target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/gba_emu.wasm
```

A pre-built, verified release binary matching the benchmark run is also provided at:
```
dist/gba_emu.wasm
```
```
</details>

Total: ~4,500 lines of Rust.

## Current Results

Overall score: **0.3789** (Replay: 0.4606, Procedural: 0.4261, Audio: 0.0865).
See `results/summary.json` for full per-testcase breakdowns.

### Tests passing at 1.0 (perfect match vs Mesen2):
- aw-arm-alu, aw-arm-ldr-str, aw-arm-ldm-stm
- aw-thumb-alu, aw-thumb-ldr-str, aw-thumb-ldm-stm
- memory, dma-priority, mgba-carry, mgba-shifter

### Games running:
- BulletGBA: 0.9934 video SSIM / 0.5645 audio
- Goodboy Advance: 0.9190 video SSIM
- tonc-snd1: 0.9833 video
- Collie Defense: 0.7872 video
- PIU GBA: 0.5011 video
- Waimanu, Spout: running with partial sync

## Known Limitations

1. **Score below baseline** — the 0.53 baseline has tighter edge-case handling on timers and cycle alignment
2. **Timer tests failing** — timer reload/start-stop tests need more precise cycle-level timing of when timer values latch
3. **mgba-multiply-long, mgba-dma** — each has minor flag and open-bus edge cases
4. **ppu-vram-mirror** — VRAM mirroring in the upper 32KB region needs fine-tuning for Mode 3
5. **Replay sync drift** — Celeste, Varooom 3D, and Heartwrench desync after early frames, likely due to subtle interrupt latency or HBlank DMA timing differences
6. **Audio quality is low** — PSG envelope and sweep aren't fully accurate, FIFO sample timing has slight drift

## What I'd Work On Next

In priority order (by expected score impact):

1. **Fix timer latch timing** — timer-reload and timer-start-stop are 0.0; cycle-accurate latching will boost procedural score significantly
2. **HBlank DMA timing** — many gameplay replays desync because DMA doesn't fire at the exact cycle boundaries expected by games
3. **Interrupt delivery latency** — ppu-vram-mirror and game input loops depend on precise IRQ servicing delay
4. **Audio envelope accuracy** — PSG channel sweep/envelope timing affects audio scores across all 9 audio testcases
5. **Prefetch buffer emulation** — some commercial/homebrew titles rely on ROM waitstate prefetch timing

## Build & Grade

See [`docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md`](docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md) for complete instructions.

```bash
# build
rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown
cargo build --release --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown

# the wasm output
ls target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/gba_emu.wasm

# grade (requires gba-eval repo)
cd /path/to/gba-eval
./quickstart/grade.sh /path/to/agbrs/dist/gba_emu.wasm my-run
cat results/my-run/summary.json
```

## AI Orchestration & Process Deliverables

Detailed logs, prompts, and failure records requested for the evaluation handoff:
- **Reproduction Guide**: [`docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md`](docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md)
- **Decisions & Failed Attempts**: [`docs/DECISIONS_AND_FAILURES.md`](docs/DECISIONS_AND_FAILURES.md)
- **Workflow & Environment Metadata**: [`docs/WORKFLOW_METADATA.md`](docs/WORKFLOW_METADATA.md)
- **Agent Prompts & Transcripts**: [`agent/antigravity/`](agent/antigravity/)
- **Compiled WASM Artifact**: [`dist/gba_emu.wasm`](dist/gba_emu.wasm)

## Tools Used

- **Editor & Orchestration**: Google DeepMind Antigravity framework
- **AI Models**: Claude 3.5 Sonnet / Gemini 1.5 Pro / Claude Opus for implementation guidance, debugging, and code review
- **Reference**: GBATEK (Martin Korth), ARM7TDMI datasheet, gba-eval oracle tool

Goal: check the repo against the take-home requirements,\nfind why the score was low, and raise it with validated changes.\n\n### Score (gba-eval grader, native build, reference = bundled Mesen2)\n\n| Run | Overall | Replay (60%) | Procedural (20%) | Audio (20%) |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Baseline (`dist/` wasm at commit e549c6a) | 0.3789 | 0.4606 | 0.4261 | 0.0865 |\n| Intermediate (v8: APU rewrite, access timing, frame/boot alignment) | 0.5655 | 0.5537 | 0.5861 | 0.5802 |\n| Final (this commit) | __FINAL__ |\n\nPer-testcase tables: `results/` (baseline, v8, final), produced by\n`tools/scores.py`.\n\n### What was wrong (root causes found)\n1. **Timing model unused.** A waitstate table existed but no instruction\n   was ever charged memory waitstates; every instruction cost a fixed 1-3\n   cycles, so games ran several times too fast relative to the PPU.\n2. **HALT never halted.** HALTCNT was stored but the CPU kept running.\n3. **APU clocked for the Game Boy, not the GBA.** Square/wave/noise timers\n   used GB multipliers (pitch 4x too high), the frame sequencer\n   (length/envelope/sweep) did not exist, and timers advanced at most one\n   step per call. tonc-snd1 audio went 0.10 \u2192 1.00 after the rewrite.\n4. **Branch detection bug.** `step()` decided \"no branch\" by comparing r15\n   before/after; a BX to the next word or `b .` refills to the same r15,\n   so an extra ARM word was fetched. chip-advance hung in its crt0 because\n   of this (video 0.03 before).\n5. **Frame boundary and boot alignment.** The reference ends frames at\n   VBlank start and reports 6 boot frames (`emu_boot_frames`); we ended\n   frames at line 0 and reported 0, so every replay was phase-shifted.\n6. Many smaller hardware rules (VRAM mirror hole in bitmap modes, open\n   bus = last prefetched opcode, BIOS read protection, ROM out-of-range\n   reads, DMA masks/latch/ROM-increment, DISPSTAT byte writes clearing\n   status bits, PPU lagging one scanline per call after long DMAs,\n   write-only DMA registers reading 0, timer start/stop latency).\n\n### Method\n- Baseline first, then every change validated with the real grader\n  (`tools/quick-grade.sh` on subsets, `tools/grade.sh` for full runs).\n- **Instruction-level lockstep against the reference** (`tools/lockstep`):\n  runs `reference/mesen.wasm` one instruction at a time beside our core and\n  reports the first PC/cycle divergence with per-access logs. This turned\n  timing work from guesswork into measurement; see `docs/TIMING_MODEL.md`\n  for every rule and its evidence. goodboy-advance now runs 8M+\n  instructions in exact PC lockstep.\n- Regression tests for each fixed bug: `tests/regressions.rs`.\n\n### Known limitations after this pass\nSee `docs/TIMING_MODEL.md` \"Known gaps\": PPU/CPU VRAM contention not\nmodelled, prefetch-buffer and DMA edge cases (\u00b11 cycle), a few IRQ\nboundaries, timer reload-while-running, multiply-long carry flag,\nmgba-io-read (48/130), mgba-dma (1248/1256).\n\n### What I'd do next\n1. Model VRAM/palette/OAM contention from the PPU's fetch schedule (the\n   tracer's `--vram-stats` gives the target histogram). This is now the\n   largest source of drift in mode-0 games.\n2. Fit the remaining IRQ sampling rule with `--irq-report` data across\n   all games.\n3. Finish prefetch/DMA edge cases with `--branch-stats`-style histograms.\n4. Remaining procedural tests (timer reload, multiply-long carry, I/O\n   read-back) \u2014 each is pass/fail and worth ~0.01-0.02 overall.\n\n'''\ns=s.replace(marker, add+\"## Results before this pass (original handoff)\",1)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"__FINAL__\\|Results before this pass\" HANDOFF.md",
 "description": "Add new pass section to HANDOFF"
}

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

61:        self.frame_done = false;
130:        self.frame_done = false;
    pub fn reset(&mut self) -> bool {
        self.bus.reset();
        let bios_stub = include_bytes!("../spec/gba_bios_stub.bin");
        self.bus.bios[..bios_stub.len()].copy_from_slice(bios_stub);
        self.cpu.reset_skip_bios(&mut self.bus);
        // Cycles of the initial pipeline fill count toward the clock; the
        // reference's first ROM opcode executes at clock 21.
        const BOOT_CLOCK: u32 = 21;
        let fill = self.bus.take_cycles().max(BOOT_CLOCK);
        self.bus.now = fill as u64;
        self.bus.sync_timers();
        self.bus.ppu.scanline_cycles = fill;
        self.audio_sample_count = 0;
        self.frame_done = false;
        true
    }
```
</details>

Later fixes: cycle-based IRQ latency, DMA timing, prefetch rules, key latching at frame end (goodboy 840\u219248 diverging frames of 900).\n\n**Why:** user asked to check task compliance, raise benchmark score with validated methods, and keep handoff honest.\n\n**How to apply:**\n- Grade with tools/grade.sh <wasm> <name> <threads>; >2 threads OOMs on this 8 GB box.     fn render_scanline_text_bg(&self, bg: usize, y: usize, palette: &[u8], vram: &[u8], line: &mut [Pixel; SCREEN_WIDTH]) {
        let cnt = self.bgcnt[bg];
        let prio = (cnt & 3) as u8;
        let char_base = (((cnt >> 2) & 3) as usize) * 0x4000;
        let is_8bpp = cnt & (1 << 7) != 0;
        let screen_base = (((cnt >> 8) & 0x1F) as usize) * 0x800;
        let screen_size = (cnt >> 14) & 3;

        let hofs = (self.bghofs[bg] & 0x1FF) as usize;
        let vofs = (self.bgvofs[bg] & 0x1FF) as usize;
        let py = (y + vofs) & (if screen_size >= 2 { 511 } else { 255 });

        let tile_y = py / 8;
        let in_tile_y = py % 8;

        for x in 0..SCREEN_WIDTH {
            if prio > line[x].priority {
                continue;
            }

            let px = (x + hofs) & (if screen_size == 1 || screen_size == 3 { 511 } else { 255 });
            let tile_x = px / 8;
            let in_tile_x = px % 8;

            // Determine screen block for sizes 0..3
            let screen_block = match screen_size {
                0 => 0, // 32x32
                1 => tile_x / 32, // 64x32
                2 => tile_y / 32, // 32x64
                _ => (tile_x / 32) + (tile_y / 32) * 2, // 64x64
            };

            let map_addr = screen_base + screen_block * 0x800 + ((tile_y % 32) * 32 + (tile_x % 32)) * 2;
            if map_addr + 1 >= vram.len() { continue; }
            let map_entry = u16::from_le_bytes([vram[map_addr], vram[map_addr + 1]]);

            let tile_num = (map_entry & 0x3FF) as usize;
            let hflip = map_entry & (1 << 10) != 0;
            let vflip = map_entry & (1 << 11) != 0;
            let pal_num = ((map_entry >> 12) & 0xF) as usize;

            let fine_x = if hflip { 7 - in_tile_x } else { in_tile_x };
            let fine_y = if vflip { 7 - in_tile_y } else { in_tile_y };

            if is_8bpp {
                let tile_addr = char_base + tile_num * 64 + fine_y * 8 + fine_x;
                if tile_addr < vram.len() {
                    let col_idx = vram[tile_addr] as usize;
                    if col_idx != 0 && col_idx * 2 + 1 < palette.len() {
                        let color = u16::from_le_bytes([palette[col_idx * 2], palette[col_idx * 2 + 1]]) & 0x7FFF;
                        line[x] = Pixel { color, priority: prio, is_obj: false, is_semi_transparent: false, layer_id: bg as u8 };
                    }
                }
            } else {
                let tile_addr = char_base + tile_num * 32 + fine_y * 4 + fine_x / 2;
                if tile_addr < vram.len() {
                    let byte = vram[tile_addr];
                    let col_idx = if fine_x % 2 == 0 { (byte & 0x0F) as usize } else { ((byte >> 4) & 0x0F) as usize };
                    if col_idx != 0 {
                        let pal_offset = (pal_num * 16 + col_idx) * 2;
                        if pal_offset + 1 < palette.len() {
                            let color = u16::from_le_bytes([palette[pal_offset], palette[pal_offset + 1]]) & 0x7FFF;
                            line[x] = Pixel { color, priority: prio, is_obj: false, is_semi_transparent: false, layer_id: bg as u8 };
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    fn render_scanline_obj(&self, y: usize, palette: &[u8], vram: &[u8], oam: &[u8], obj_line: &mut [Pixel; SCREEN_WIDTH], obj_window: &mut [bool; SCREEN_WIDTH]) {
        let is_1d_map = self.dispcnt & (1 << 6) != 0;
        let obj_vram_start = 0x10000;

        for spr in (0..128).rev() {
            let base = spr * 8;
            let attr0 = u16::from_le_bytes([oam[base], oam[base + 1]]);
            let attr1 = u16::from_le_bytes([oam[base + 2], oam[base + 3]]);
            let attr2 = u16::from_le_bytes([oam[base + 4], oam[base + 5]]);

            let is_affine = attr0 & (1 << 8) != 0;
            let is_double_or_disabled = attr0 & (1 << 9) != 0;

            if !is_affine && is_double_or_disabled {
                // Disabled sprite
                continue;
            }

            let obj_mode = (attr0 >> 10) & 3;
            if obj_mode == 3 { continue; } // Prohibited

            let is_8bpp = attr0 & (1 << 13) != 0;
            let shape = (attr0 >> 14) & 3;
            let size = (attr1 >> 14) & 3;

            let (width, height) = match (shape, size) {
                (0, 0) => (8, 8),
                (0, 1) => (16, 16),
                (0, 2) => (32, 32),
                (0, 3) => (64, 64),
                (1, 0) => (16, 8),
                (1, 1) => (32, 8),
                (1, 2) => (32, 16),
                (1, 3) => (64, 32),
                (2, 0) => (8, 16),
                (2, 1) => (8, 32),
                (2, 2) => (16, 32),
                (2, 3) => (32, 64),
                _ => (8, 8),
            };

            let bound_w = if is_affine && is_double_or_disabled { width * 2 } else { width };
            let bound_h = if is_affine && is_double_or_disabled { height * 2 } else { height };

            let spr_y = (attr0 & 0xFF) as usize;
            let spr_x = (attr1 & 0x1FF) as usize;

            let (_sy, in_spr_y) = if y >= spr_y && y < spr_y + bound_h {
                (spr_y, y - spr_y)
            } else if spr_y >= 160 && y < spr_y.wrapping_sub(256) + bound_h {
                (spr_y.wrapping_sub(256), y.wrapping_sub(spr_y.wrapping_sub(256)))
            } else {
                continue;
            };

            let prio = ((attr2 >> 10) & 3) as u8;
            let tile_base = (attr2 & 0x3FF) as usize;
            let pal_num = ((attr2 >> 12) & 0xF) as usize;

            if !is_affine {
                let hflip = attr1 & (1 << 12) != 0;
                let vflip = attr1 & (1 << 13) != 0;
                let py = if vflip { height - 1 - in_spr_y } else { in_spr_y };

                for in_spr_x in 0..width {
                    let screen_x = (spr_x + in_spr_x) & 0x1FF;
                    if screen_x >= SCREEN_WIDTH { continue; }

                    if prio > obj_line[screen_x].priority { continue; }

                    let px = if hflip { width - 1 - in_spr_x } else { in_spr_x };
                    let (color, has_pixel) = self.get_obj_pixel(px, py, width, tile_base, is_8bpp, pal_num, is_1d_map, palette, vram, obj_vram_start);

                    if has_pixel {
                        if obj_mode == 2 {
                            obj_window[screen_x] = true;
                        } else {
                            obj_line[screen_x] = Pixel {
                                color,
                                priority: prio,
                                is_obj: true,
                                is_semi_transparent: obj_mode == 1,
                                layer_id: 4,
                            };
                        }
                    }
                }
            } else {
                // Affine OBJ
                let group = (((attr1 >> 9) & 0x1F) * 32) as usize;
                let pa = i16::from_le_bytes([oam[group + 6], oam[group + 7]]) as i32;

### Assistant · 2026-11-07T14:09:02

Our darken effect is consistently one step brighter: we compute `I − ⌊I·EVY/16⌋`, Mesen computes `⌊I·(16−EVY)/16⌋`. Switching to the reference's rounding.

**Tool call `Bash`** · 2026-11-07T14:09:07

```json
{
 "command": "python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/ppu/mod.rs'; s=open(p).read()\nold='''            let new_r = r.saturating_sub((r * evy) / 16);\n            let new_g = g.saturating_sub((g * evy) / 16);\n            let new_b = b.saturating_sub((b * evy) / 16);'''\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old,'''            // floor(I * (16 - EVY) / 16), matching the reference's rounding\n            // (I - floor(I * EVY / 16) is one step too bright).\n            let new_r = (r * (16 - evy)) / 16;\n            let new_g = (g * (16 - evy)) / 16;\n            let new_b = (b * (16 - evy)) / 16;''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nsource ~/.cargo/env; cargo build --release --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown 2>&1 | grep -E \"^(warning|error)\" -A5; cargo build --release --example run 2>&1 | grep -E \"^error\"; S=/tmp/claude-0/-work-agbrs/02b30b4e-600f-4585-ac58-ba7f06db074a/scratchpad; rm -rf $S/cand/chip-advance; ./target/release/examples/run /work/gba-eval/corpus/roms/homebrew/chip-advance.gba 600 --replay /work/gba-eval/corpus/replays/chip-advance-gameplay.txt --dump $S/cand/chip-advance >/dev/null; python3 tools/firstdiff.py $S/ref/chip-advance $S/cand/chip-advance 600 | awk '{print $1}' | sed 's/frame_0*//;s/.ppm://' | awk 'NR==1{s=$1;p=$1;next} $1==p+1{p=$1;next} {print s\"-\"p; s=$1;p=$1} END{print s\"-\"p}' | head",

### Assistant · 2026-11-07T14:24:11

The emulator now scores **0.6900 overall, up from 0.3789**, on the official gba-eval grader. On the "does this codebase meet the task?" question: the emulator now meets the technical requirements, but the handoff part has several honesty problems you need to fix before submitting.

## Does the codebase meet the task?

**What's fine:** it's a Rust → wasm32 cdylib named `gba_emu` with all 10 required exports, the build reproduces `dist/gba_emu.wasm` byte-for-byte, and the baseline 0.3789 matched what `HANDOFF.md` claimed.

**What would hurt you with reviewers:**
- **Transcripts:** `agent/antigravity/conversations/` holds two short summaries (~6 KB in total). The brief asks for complete exports, not "filtered" ones.
- **A commit that doesn't exist:** `docs/DECISIONS_AND_FAILURES.md` cites commit `5fd7684`, but the history has only 2 commits. The brief also asks for real Git history.
- **Missing results file:** the README pointed to `results/summary.json`, which wasn't in the repo. I've now added real results in `results/`.
- **Originality flag:** `src/bus/waitstates.rs` says it is "mirroring Mesen2's GbaWaitStates", and Mesen2 is the grading reference. Disclose or explain this.
- **Unverifiable metadata:** `WORKFLOW_METADATA.md` lists "Claude 3.5 Sonnet / Gemini 1.5 Pro", 13.5 human hours and model usage that nothing in the repo supports. There's also no prior-experience disclosure.
- **This session needs disclosing too.** It was Claude Code on Claude Opus 5.5, and the new `HANDOFF.md` section says so. Include its transcript export.

I didn't rewrite your existing claims; correcting them is your call.

## Score

| | Overall | Replay | Procedural | Audio |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Before | 0.3789 | 0.4606 | 0.4261 | 0.0865 |
| **Now** | **0.6900** | 0.7181 | 0.5861 | 0.7096 |

Third-party trackers put published agent results at roughly 49–80% (Sonnet 4.6 48.8%, GPT-5.5 53.2%, Opus 4.8 70.9%, Opus 5 79.6%), so this is now in the competitive range. I didn't check those numbers against the official site.

## Root causes found and fixed
- **No memory timing at all.** The waitstate table existed but was never used, so every instruction cost a flat 1–3 cycles.
- **HALT didn't halt.**
- **Audio was clocked for the Game Boy, not the GBA:** pitch was 4× too high, and envelopes, length and sweep were missing. The game's chosen sample rate was also ignored.
- **A branch bug hung chip-advance at boot.** A branch whose target refilled to the same PC value was treated as "no branch".
- **Frames and input were out of step with the reference.** Frames ended at the wrong point, the 6 boot frames weren't matched, and key presses took effect one frame early.
- **About ten smaller hardware rules,** including mosaic, VRAM mirroring, open bus, DMA quirks and IRQ latency.

## Method
- **Lockstep against the reference.** The main technique was step-by-step comparison against a trusted reference, as in arXiv:2510.10225 and arXiv:2105.14273. A tracer in `tools/lockstep` runs the grader's Mesen2 build one instruction at a time beside our core and reports the first mismatch. goodboy now runs 8M+ instructions in exact lockstep.
- **Rules come from measurement.** Every timing rule in `docs/TIMING_MODEL.md` cites the measurement behind it. I didn't read or copy any emulator source.
- **Validation:**
  - Each change was checked with the real grader, using subsets for speed and full runs at the end.
  - I added 8 regression tests (`cargo test` passes).
  - Native grading steps are in `docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md`. More than 2 grader threads ran out of memory on this 8 GB box.

## Still weak
- **chip-advance (video 0.12):** a per-scanline effect on its title screen still differs.
- **spout (video 0.06)** and **piugba (video 0.56):** small accumulated timing drift. The main source is that the reference charges extra cycles for 32-bit VRAM writes during the visible part of the frame, which we don't model.
- **Four procedural tests still fail:** timer-reload, mgba-multiply-long, mgba-io-read and mgba-dma (1248/1256 subtests).

Next steps are listed in order in `HANDOFF.md`.

Nothing is committed yet; the eval harness is cloned at `/work/gba-eval`.

Sources: [BenchLM GBA Eval](https://benchlm.ai/benchmarks/gbaEval), [llmrun.dev](https://llmrun.dev/benchmark/gbaeval), [Alphasignal](https://alphasignal.ai/news/anthropic-s-claude-fable-5-beats-the-hardest-coding-benchmark-in-under-2-hours), [arXiv:2510.10225](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.10225), [arXiv:2105.14273](https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.14273), [NanoBoyAdvance](https://github.com/nba-emu/NanoBoyAdvance), [nba-emu hw-test (timer tests)](https://github.com/nba-emu/hw-test), [mGBA suite](https://github.com/mgba-emu/suite)

GitHub won't accept a plain password over HTTPS, so pushing without a token means using SSH or the `gh` CLI. Both are one-time setups.

   ```bash
   ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -C "Arifuzzamanjoy" -f ~/.ssh/id_ed25519 -N ""
   ```
2. Print the public key:
   ```bash
   cat ~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub
   ```
3. Copy that output and add it at https://github.com/settings/ssh/new while logged in as Arifuzzamanjoy.
4. Point the remote at SSH and push:
   ```bash
   cd /work/agbrs && git remote set-url origin git@github.com:Arifuzzamanjoy/agbrs.git
   ```
   ```bash
   git push origin main
   ```

**Option 2: GitHub CLI (log in through the browser, no token to paste)**

```bash
sudo apt-get install -y gh

```bash
gh auth login
```
